<meta>This page consists of decorative block-printed paper, typically used as an endpaper or cover for the manuscript. There is no handwritten or printed text on this page.</meta>

->**Decorative Block-Printed Pattern**<-

This page features a repetitive botanical and floral design on a cream-colored background, characteristic of historical bookbinding. The pattern is organized into a diamond-shaped lattice created by thin, dark grey diagonal lines that resemble thorny vines or branches.

Within and along this lattice are small, colorful clusters consisting of three specific elements:
*   A larger reddish-pink flower bud or berry
*   A smaller, light pink bud
*   A green, heart-shaped leaf or fruit

The print has a slightly irregular, hand-stamped appearance, which is a hallmark of <term>block printing<note>A manual printing technique where a design is carved into a wooden block, inked, and pressed onto the paper.</note></term>. This type of <term>decorative paper<note>Often called "dominotiere" paper when produced in this style, it was widely used for book covers and endpapers in the 18th century.</note></term> was often used to protect the inner pages of a manuscript while providing an aesthetic finish to the volume.

<summary>A decorative endpaper featuring a hand-stamped botanical pattern with a diamond-shaped vine lattice and pink floral buds.</summary>
<keywords>floral pattern, block print, decorative paper, bookbinding, botanical design, endpaper</keywords>

--- Page 2 ---
<meta>This page continues the display of decorative block-printed paper used for the manuscript's binding, following the previous endpaper.</meta>

->**Decorative Lattice Floral Pattern**<-

This page displays a repetitive botanical design set against a cream-colored background. The pattern is organized into diagonal rows, creating a rhythmic, lattice-like visual effect that covers the entire surface.

The design features:
*   Thin, dark greyish-black stems marked with small, thorn-like spikes.
*   Clusters of small, round reddish-pink flower buds.
*   Larger, heart-shaped green leaves.

This type of <term>floral pattern<note>Commonly used in 18th and 19th-century bookbinding to provide a decorative finish to the interior covers.</note></term> was typically produced using <term>block printing<note>A manual process where a design is carved into a wooden block, inked, and then stamped onto the paper.</note></term>. The <term>lattice motif<note>A pattern consisting of crossed strips, usually in a diagonal or diamond shape.</note></term> seen here is a classic example of <term>decorative paper<note>Often used as endpapers or temporary covers for pamphlets and manuscripts before they received a permanent leather or cloth binding.</note></term> intended to enhance the aesthetic appeal of a hand-bound volume.

<summary>A decorative endpaper featuring a repeating botanical motif of thorny stems, pink flower buds, and green leaves in a diagonal lattice arrangement.</summary>
<keywords>floral pattern, bookbinding, decorative paper, lattice motif, botanical design, block printing</keywords>

<meta>This page serves as the frontispiece for the manuscript, following the decorative floral endpapers that line the inner cover. It introduces the thematic focus of the work through a complex symbolic emblem.</meta>

<insert>
M

<image-desc>An illuminated emblem is pasted onto the center of the page. The emblem features a central oval medallion bordered by a green serpent biting its own tail (the ouroboros). Within the medallion, against a blue background, a white pelican is perched upon a golden crescent moon. Above the bird, golden rays descend from the top edge of the oval. Below the moon, a white cube is decorated with four red roses. The central motif is encircled by a circular inscription in gold-colored capital letters.</image-desc>

->**Hermetic Philosophy**<- <note>original Latin: "PHILOSOPHIA HERMETICA"</note>
</insert>

<note>The symbols displayed here are cornerstone images of <term>Hermeticism<note>A philosophical tradition based on writings attributed to Hermes Trismegistus, focusing on the relationship between the divine, the cosmos, and humanity.</note></term>. The <term>ouroboros<note>The serpent eating its own tail, representing infinity, the unity of all things, and the eternal cycle of nature.</note></term> encloses the <term>pelican<note>In alchemical symbolism, the pelican "vulning" (piercing) its breast to feed its young represents the stage of "multiplication" or self-sacrifice in the Great Work.</note></term>. The <term>crescent moon<note>Associated with the metal silver and the "feminine" principle in alchemy.</note></term> and the <term>cube<note>Representing the physical element of Earth or the stability of the material world.</note></term> adorned with four <term>roses<note>A common Rosicrucian symbol representing the unfolding of the spirit within the material realm.</note></term> suggest a synthesis of spiritual and earthly elements.</note>

<vocab>Hermetic Philosophy, pelican, ouroboros, rose, cube, hermeticism</vocab>

## Printed in the year 1710.

<summary>This is the title page of a 1710 theological work by Nikolaus Tscheer, which uses the historical "failings" of the ancient Israelites as a moral and spiritual mirror for contemporary Christians. The text is written from the perspective of a humble "pilgrim" and calls for deep self-examination and conversion.</summary>
<keywords>Nikolaus Tscheer, Pietism, 1710, biblical typology, Israel, 1 Corinthians, repentance, spiritual pilgrimage, 18th-century theology</keywords>

--- Page 8 ---
<meta>This page begins the dedication, addressed to "Dear Brothers." It establishes a typological connection between the biblical Israelites and the current readers, warning of spiritual failures.</meta>

# Dedication.

## Dear Brothers in the Grace of GOD through JESUS Christ!

Those prototypes <note>German: *Fürbilder* — refers to "types" or biblical patterns that prefigure future events</note> of the deviations of that Israel according to the flesh were written by Paul and dedicated as a warning to all those Israelites according to the Spirit upon whom the ends of the world-ages <note>German: *Welt-Ewigkeiten* — a term suggesting the final eras of time or aeons</note> have come. But who has come closer to the end of the world, and become more similar to those prefiguring Israelites, than all of us? For we have been called in the last of these days through the living and inwardly powerful word of GOD to a change of heart <note>German: *Aenderung unseres Sinnes* — a translation of the Greek *metanoia*, or repentance</note>, and have been drawn and led by our dear LORD toward the true Sabbath and the essential rest with GOD. 

Therefore, no one should more rightly take Paul’s faithful warning to heart and examine themselves with sincerity than we, who have inwardly experienced almost all the powers of GOD’s grace and the secret work of redemption. We have done so in order to depart from the spiritual Sodom and Egypt of our corrupted nature and to strive for the true rest of union with GOD. Yet, on the contrary, we have mostly followed those prefigured footsteps of evil desires, idolatry, and deviations through our own disobedience, unfaithfulness, and apostasy. Therefore, no one has better or greater cause to take the dedicated warning of Paul to heart, for the examination of his own conscience—

<summary>In this dedication, the author compares the spiritual journey of contemporary believers to the biblical Israelites, warning that those who have received God's grace are just as prone to the sins of idolatry and disobedience.</summary>
<keywords>dedication, Paul the Apostle, Israel, typology, repentance, spiritual Sabbath, union with God, corruption of nature</keywords>

--- Page 9 ---
<meta>This page continues the dedication from page 3, concluding the long sentence regarding the examination of conscience and the need for spiritual awakening.</meta>

-> Dedication. <-

perceive <note>The German word *wahrnehmen* (to perceive/observe) begins on the previous page as "wahr-" and concludes here with "nehmen."</note> / as we: Whether we would indeed let ourselves be raised up again from our fall through the still-active grace of the faithful discipline of the Holy Spirit, be guided aright out of error, and be awakened from the deep sleep of fleshly security <note>German: *fleischlicher Sicherheit* — a theological term describing a state of false peace or complacency where one feels safe despite being in sin</note>, so that we—in joyful faith and willing obedience—might faithfully and zealously continue our journey <note>German: *Wandel* — way of life or spiritual conduct</note> out of the corruption of our nature, into which we have mostly retreated again, through earnest self-denial and the putting to death of the self; especially since all of us—provided we are not entirely frozen in the blindness of self-love through error—see before our eyes how a terrifying thunderstorm and dark-black clouds of the judgments of GOD are drawing over our inhabited globe on every side, threatening this world with—if not the total end—at least a cruel destruction. 

We, however, who should not only be fortified in prayer and faith for our own sakes, but also stand in the breach <note>German: *in den Riß stehen* — a biblical metaphor from Ezekiel 22:30, referring to those who intercede for others to stay God's judgment</note> as a priestly race <note>A reference to the "royal priesthood" mentioned in 1 Peter 2:9</note> in faithful intercession for the whole human race, instead lie scattered in our own self-will, making ourselves partakers in the sins of this world and the abominations of the nations, and deviating more and more from the true rest of GOD through our own strivings <note>German: *eigene Würckungen* — refers to human efforts or "self-workings" that interfere with divine grace</note>. May the mercy of GOD bring our misery poignantly to our hearts, raise up our tired knees and our weary hands again, and help us all back to the right path through the grace and power of the Holy Spirit, Amen!

<summary>The author concludes his appeal for spiritual renewal, warning that the "dark clouds" of God's judgment are gathering over the world and calling on believers to return to their roles as intercessors.</summary>
<keywords>Holy Spirit, repentance, divine judgment, self-denial, intercession, spiritual complacency, priesthood of all believers</keywords>

When we consider / in what a barren state so-called Christianity was still in twenty and more years ago / we might well compare it to the state of Abraham / since at that time the Sarah of the higher Jerusalem <note>original: *Sara des oberen Jerusalems* — A reference to Galatians 4:26, where the "Jerusalem above" is described as a free woman and the mother of believers</note> / the mother of us all / was for a while as if locked away in her birth-mother <note>The author uses *Gebähr-Mutter* (womb/birth-mother) metaphorically to describe the spiritual capacity for new life within the institutional church</note> / namely in this lower Church / and the maidservant Hagar / the outer / visible and fleshly Church / gave birth to the scorner Ishmael into bondage and a legalistic, literal service <note>German: *gesätzlichen buchstäblichen Dienst* — Religious practice focused strictly on the "letter" of the law rather than its spiritual heart</note> / but the true seed of the promise remained for the most part locked away and hidden in the loins of Abraham. And although here and there some hearts were pregnant with the new birth / the children came as it were only to the point of birth / but there was no strength to break through and deliver: Until GOD sent some angels <note>In this context, "angels" (German: *Engel*) often refers to messengers or prophetic voices—reformers and spiritual leaders sent by God</note> and faithful witnesses of the truth / with whom the Holy Spirit worked in their testimony / so that the birth-mother of Sarah was opened in many hearts—which were as if dead—for the conception of the living seed of the promise / and was blessed with the birth of the new man; So that throughout almost all of Europe there was no kingdom / religion, or form of worship / where the great and powerful Angel of the Covenant <note>A title for Christ, derived from Malachi 3:1</note> did not powerfully stir the living Word for the birth of the new creature <note>German: *neuen Creatur* — See 2 Corinthians 5:17 regarding the spiritual transformation of the individual</note> in many hearts. And although Ishmael—born from the will of man and the powers of nature / the son of Hagar who gives birth into bondage / I say / the fleshly / purely literal and legalistic so-called Christian / in all

Yet they would do neither the works of *Abraham* nor those of his faith/ but rather sought to fulfill the works of the devil/ and tried to stifle the true seed of the promise/ it happened/ that GOD in many places/ without anyone being able to say/ how it came to pass/ through His secret/ yet soul- and spirit-piercing Word, birthed children to *Abraham* in an instant <note>A reference to Matthew 3:9, where John the Baptist claims God can raise children for Abraham from stones.</note> from the hard stones of fleshly and earthly-minded hearts and blind worldly people. Praised and blessed be the immeasurable power of the riches of God's grace with eternal gratitude from all the children of *Abraham* in the entire lower and upper house of *Sarah*!

And as that *Israel* according to the flesh, through God’s blessing-rich omnipotence, multiplied from seventy to six hundred thousand souls amidst all the hard and heavy servitude in *Egypt*; so it happened here as well/ although the number is not so explicitly before our eyes/ that our modern-day *Israelites* <note>The author uses "Israelites" here as a synonym for the burgeoning group of true believers or Pietists.</note> under all the *Egyptian* burden/ mockery/ derision/ persecution, and robbery of goods/ broke out in such a way/ that even the wisest counselors of *Pharaoh* were made fools/ and could not fathom the secret work of God’s Wisdom/ as to how it came to pass/ that the <term>Pietists</term> <note>original: *Pietisten* — Initially a derisive term for those in the 17th-century reform movement who emphasized personal holiness; the author notes here that they were "named in mockery."</note>/ for so they were named in mockery/ increased so abundantly in all places in such a short time: Where one thought/ that there were only one or a couple/ it happened/ that they unexpectedly broke forth in bands and troops. Indeed, Pharaoh—for so I name the spirit of this world—whether he *rules* <note>original: "prædominire"</note> in the authorities or in the **clergy**, seeks to drown the new creature in the waters of vanity! He does this both from within, through various temptations, worries about the belly <note>The author uses "Bauch-sorgen" to refer to anxieties over physical needs and material survival.</note>, lusts, and desires of the flesh, as well as from without, sometimes through all kinds of harsh decrees, commands, punishments, bonds, and prisons, and at other times through a thousand types of cunning and flattery. But GOD nonetheless faithfully helped many a heart that was in the anguish of labor to bring forth the new birth. How many a dear soul at this very hour can tell of great wonders, and of how faithful and true GOD proved Himself to be in their anxieties! Yes, whereas previously many a **Rachel** had to die in childbirth because it went hard for her, it happened here that the souls became hardy, like the **Hebrew women** <note>A reference to Exodus 1:19, where the Hebrew midwives told Pharaoh that the Jewish women were so vigorous they gave birth before the midwives could arrive to kill the infants.</note>, and the new birth broke forth from within before the midwives appointed by Pharaoh for murder could arrive, or before any outward help or means could be prescribed for the destruction of the birth.

The many hard trials, temptations from within, and persecutions from without, drove many a soul—indeed, often entire households and assemblies—together to pray to GOD with a single heart, asking that He might send them His **Kinsman-Redeemer** <note>original: "Goel"; a Hebrew term for a relative who redeems a person or property. In this context, it refers to Christ as the spiritual redeemer.</note> and Savior. They asked that He might redeem them not so much from the outward cross <note>The "cross" here refers to external suffering or persecution.</note>, as from sin and the prison of **selfhood** <term>Eigenheit: a term used in Pietist and mystical literature to describe the state of being trapped in one's own ego and selfish will rather than being surrendered to God.</term>. This the faithful GOD also often did quite visibly, proving to the prince of this world and all the **Egyptians** with many signs and wonders that He had specially chosen this people to serve Him alone in the freedom and joy of the spirit. And although the stories of wonders that occurred among them were not perhaps as visible and manifest to everyone as those were to the **Egyptians** of old, nevertheless, such things have happened here and there that have caused no little wonder and consternation among many people of the world, even those who are **intellectually-wise**. Whereby, however, Pharaoh and his

<summary>The author uses the story of the Hebrew midwives to explain how the "new spiritual birth" survives the persecutions of the world. He argues that internal trials and external oppression drive believers to seek a deeper "Redeemer" who frees them from the prison of their own ego.</summary>
<keywords>Pharaoh, new birth, clergy, selfhood, Goel, redemption, Hebrew women, spiritual exodus, persecution</keywords>

# 8

...wise counselors only became all the more stubborn and hardened, so that they held the wonders of GOD and the mighty powers of His grace to be either merely the natural effects of souls and their **temperaments** <term>original: "Complexionen"; referring to the four humors or the physical makeup of a person's character</term>, or even blasphemed them as sorcery and devilish deceit—especially where one thing or another was mimicked here and there by the **Egyptian sorcerer-spirits** <note>The author uses "Egypt" as an allegory for the worldly or corrupted state church, and "sorcerer-spirits" for those who use intellectual or ritual trickery to oppose spiritual truth.</note>.

My intention is not to tell here at length the many occurrences that happened here and there among these people—which are not hidden to most who are known among them: may GOD let such things remain unforgotten in all our hearts! Nothing has brought me more into wonder than that among this **little flock** <note>A common term for a small community of true believers, often used by Pietist groups.</note> so uncommonly great a light and knowledge has dawned, and the glory of the Wisdom of GOD shone forth from many such simple hearts; the likes of which has not happened among Christians in many hundreds of years. 

Or where, since the times of the first Christians, has such a glorious light and knowledge been revealed as lies before the eyes of all the world in the recorded oral and written testimonies? While, on the contrary, such a thick blindness and darkness fell upon the **Egyptians** and their sorcerers (I speak of the earthly-minded, fleshly **belly-priests** <term>original: "Bauch-Pfaffen"; a derogatory term for clergy more interested in their own physical comfort and salaries than in spiritual matters</term> and their idolatrous following) that they, as it were, can no longer find their own dogmas and orders, with which they are otherwise entirely covered, and cannot use them against the least and simplest confessors of the truth. 

Indeed, what they otherwise consider anti-Christian and damnable in the **Roman Catholic religion** within their own sects—whenever their followers were perhaps persecuted by them—that they themselves have carried out on others with the sharpest zeal through the **secular sword** <note>The use of state power and legal force to punish religious dissent.</note>. With this, however, they achieved nothing more than that they marked themselves—both in their consciences and on their foreheads—with the infallible sign of the **Cainite murderous spirit** <note>Refers to the spirit of Cain, who murdered his brother Abel; used here to describe those who persecute their spiritual brothers.</note>

<meta>catchword: the</meta>

<summary>The author describes how the worldly authorities and "belly-priests" (corrupt clergy) grew hardened against the spiritual revival, viewing divine grace as mere psychology or sorcery. Despite this, the author marvels at the profound spiritual wisdom revealed to the "simple hearts" of the dissenting community.</summary>
<keywords>Pharaoh's counselors, spiritual blindness, belly-priests, little flock, persecution, Cainite spirit, light and knowledge</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the narrative from page 8. ...believed, were branded as followers of the **Antichrist** <note>In this context, the "Antichrist" refers not just to a single figure, but to the corrupt institutional church systems that the author believes oppose the true spirit of Christ.</note>; and these oppressed little folk were driven into the footsteps of the suffering and patience of JESUS, to follow our dear Lord and Master in true sanctification and **conformity to His cross** <term>original: "Gleichförmigkeit seines Creutzes"; a theological concept of modeling one's life exactly after the suffering of Christ</term>, even unto death in joyful obedience. Indeed, such darkness and stubborn blindness broke in upon these **Egyptians** <note>The author continues the allegory of "Egypt" representing the worldly power and the "Pharaohs" representing those who enslave or persecute the true people of God.</note> that they feared a violent assault from these otherwise peaceful, weaponless, and powerless people. They were so plagued by the ghosts of their own imagination that they called upon the authorities and the **secular arm** <term>original: "weltlichen Arm"; the use of civil or military force to enforce religious dictates</term> for protection and help. For this reason, in some places, the cities, gates, and houses were guarded day and night with armed hands because of only one or two peace-loving persons.

And while the enemies were often seized by a hidden anxiety and secret fear, so great a light, joy, and peace dawned upon the confessors of the truth that they prayed to GOD for their enemies and praised His name that they were worthy to suffer for His sake. Indeed, where they had perhaps clashed with someone through untimely zeal, they asked for mercy; but where someone suffered in quiet patience without seeking self-revenge for the sake of the truth, there the vengeance of GOD manifested itself clearly upon many an enemy of the truth as an example to others. How many died a sudden death! They met a terrifying end! They endured uncommonly painful illnesses! They became **rabid like dogs**! They were attacked by worms while still alive! And other ways GOD's wrath was exercised, so that in several places people were glad when they could be rid of these people in a polite manner, and when they moved away from their locations of their own accord.

GOD, according to His unsearchable wisdom, indeed very often permits many a poor soul to wander about in great drought and darkness, in spiritual hunger and thirst, and in many kinds of crooked paths, so that Pharaoh and his counselors, according to their fleshly...

<summary>This page describes the paradox of persecution where powerful authorities feared a small, non-violent group of believers, resulting in heavy military guarding. It also details the "divine vengeance" or gruesome fates that allegedly befell those who persecuted the peaceful community.</summary>

...human wisdom <note>The text likely completes the word "menschlichen" (human) from the previous page.</note> consider it so—as if one were lost in the land, that God had forsaken them, and the wilderness had shut them in (**Exodus 14:3**) <note>The author cites the Second Book of Moses, known as Exodus, referring to Pharaoh's belief that the Israelites were trapped before the Red Sea.</note>, so that they might easily be captured again into their former servitude, or even return of their own accord to perform their former services. For this reason, the spirit of this world assaults such poor souls with countless temptations from without and within, so that they know neither which way to turn. But where such an oppressed heart takes its refuge in God and surrenders itself to His most holy will in life and death; how does one not experience daily how the **Faithful Shepherd** <note>A common Pietist reference to Jesus Christ as the protector of the soul.</note> in His power proves Himself so gloriously to such a poor little sheep! How has He not often stilled our anxious soul with His voice of love! With His divine power of love, He has fought for us, without our help, against our enemies! He divided the roaring and foaming waters that came against us! That which threatened death, He made into a firm wall to protect our lives! How has He not paved many a beautiful path for us through the depths of the sea, so that we walked through its depths as if through a beautiful green field, like horses in the pasture, and jumped like lambs, praising and singing to the Lord who has so wonderfully redeemed us! (**Wisdom of Solomon 19:9**) <note>A reference to the deuterocanonical Book of Wisdom, describing the Exodus as a joyful crossing.</note>. It is beyond description how, in every place one traveled—in Lower and Upper Germany, Saxony, Holland, Switzerland—one heard people praising and recounting the wondrously great deeds of God that had occurred there. From England, France, Italy, and other lands, one likewise heard of uncommonly great movements. From such glorious works of God’s redemption and rescue, so many beautiful songs of praise broke forth from liberated hearts that great songbooks were filled and collected as a testimony for those of weak faith—showing how many a poor, imprisoned soul God so wonderfully [led] through the **house of bondage** <term>original: "Dienst-Hause"; a biblical term for Egypt and, by extension, any state of spiritual or physical enslavement</term> and all temptations...

<summary>This page employs the biblical imagery of the Exodus to describe the spiritual liberation of believers, reporting that a great religious awakening accompanied by many new hymns is spreading across Europe.</summary>

(❁:) 11 (:❁)

led: The Spirit of Wisdom united entire choirs of hearts full of joy, who played to the Lord with kettledrums and songs of praise.

And so that this little group <note>The "little flock" or "Häuflein" is a common Pietist self-designation for their community.</note> would not mix in any way with the children of this world, the faithfulness of God marked them like little sheep, so that—even before they knew it themselves—they were recognized by the world as a peculiar kind of people; even in places where some were unknown by face, they were nonetheless immediately regarded as such and such, and called <term>Pietists</term> <note>Originally used as a derisive label, "Pietist" refers to the 17th-18th century reform movement within Lutheranism emphasizing individual piety, small group study, and lived devotion over formal dogma.</note>: to say nothing of the secret friendship and brotherhood they immediately shared whenever they met, even if they had never seen one another in their entire lives, and were otherwise by nature of completely different <term>Complexion</term> <note>In early modern medicine, this refers to a person’s temperament or physical constitution based on the four humors.</note>, country, status, religion, and other circumstances; so that not only they, but the world itself, had to marvel and experience how the Lord separates Israel and Egypt <note>A biblical metaphor from Exodus 11:7, used here to describe the distinction between the "godly" and the "worldly."</note>. Besides this, it cannot be denied that—just as with those children of Israel according to the flesh—a great deal of rabble and unbroken worldly people, loose chatterers and deceivers, followed along out of many false and evil intentions; these have caused the true children of God much unrest, hindrance, and suffering from the beginning until this day; although this, too, has had to work toward the greater sanctification and the killing of spiritual pride <note>original: "Hoffart"</note> in upright and honest hearts.

If we were to follow those Israelites according to the flesh further, and trace the footsteps of God’s goodness that led them, we would find many a landmark <note>original: "Marckmal"</note> among us. Wherever the waters in the desert were bitter and thirst was strong in many, how often has God split hard rocks and touched the most hardened hearts through His miraculous hand in such a way that they poured out the sweet waters of help and comfort, and dissolved into physical and spiritual charity toward those who were half-fainting! those who, through a stronger

Our external water-baptism / and all the powers / that perhaps fall from heaven upon our souls / whether they be by day / in the state of rational enlightenment from the images and letters of the Scripture / or by night / when we wish to let ourselves be led and guided by others / and do not settle ourselves into the fire and light of **GOD** with our own blindness / these occur in a brief and swift act <note>original: "actu"</note> / and often nothing remains of them but the memory. II. The second Baptism / which occurred in a single act <note>original: "actu"</note> once and for all / was in the Sea. This baptism was the greatest external trial of faith for Israel / and the most glorious revelation of the omnipotence of **GOD**; for this reason / the passage through the Red Sea is praised in the most excellent way throughout the whole of Scripture / especially in the Psalms of David. It is remarkable / that the children of Israel / in order to travel to Canaan / would not have needed / to travel either through the Red Sea / or through the wildernesses / but would have had a closer and better way through the land of the Philistines. For so the text speaks in Moses: **GOD** led them not on the road through the Philistines' land / which was the closest; for **GOD** thought / the people might repent / when they saw the conflict / and turn back to Egypt: therefore he made the people take a detour / the way of the same wilderness by the Sea of Reeds <note>original: "Schilff-Meer"; commonly known as the Red Sea</note>. 2nd Book of Moses XIII, 17. 18. <note>Exodus 13:17-18</note>

This entire detour through the wilderness and through the sea was the trial of Israel / for the mortification <term>Abtödtung: a mystical term for the "killing off" of the selfish ego and worldly attachments to make room for the divine</term> of their Egyptian nature and for the sanctification of an eternal peace-rest in and with **GOD**. Here I wish / that all those / who make so much of external water-baptism / would examine themselves well / to see if they would trust themselves to endure such a baptism? Israel had behind them the pursuing army of Pharaoh <note>original: "Pharaonis"</note> with so many thousands of raging enemies; before them they saw nothing but the roaring waves of the sea coming against them: and were thus surrounded on all sides / behind and before / with pure death-agony and danger. Was not the entire host of Israel here baptized through and through / from within and without / into death? Since they then also considered themselves entirely lost in regard to their lives and all their own powers / saying: Were there no graves in Egypt / that you had to lead us away / to die in the wilderness? <note>Exodus 14:11</note> Nevertheless / they were strengthened by Moses / so that they stepped into the sea; and as **GOD** the

<meta>This page continues the discussion of manna as a spiritual type of Christ. The previous page ended with an explanation of the Hebrew name for manna, "Man hu," which the author now connects to the testimony of Moses.</meta>

Moses testifies. This **Manna** was not merely a figure or image; rather, Paul calls it a spiritual food <note>1 Corinthians 10:3.</note> which not only satisfies the body but also strengthens the soul, and infuses <note>The original uses the term *tingirt*, from the Latin *tingere*, an alchemical and mystical term meaning to permeate or transform the essence of a substance.</note> its essences with angelic <note>The German *Englische* can mean "English," but in this theological context, it always refers to *Engelisch* (angelic).</note> qualities and properties. Thus, just as the Israelites partook of one bread in the manna together with the angels and the mighty ones of **God**, they were also united and incorporated with them into a single body for the praise of **God**, until they had passed through the angelic dominion and attained fellowship with **God** and his Son. It is noteworthy that the manna did not fall inside the camp; rather, all those who wished to gather it had to go outside the camp, as is seen from the testimony of Moses in Exodus 16.

Despite the fact that the Israelites enjoyed such food every day for forty years—so that, if they had directed their true soul-desire toward this spiritual food at the same time as their physical hunger, they would undoubtedly have been elevated into an angelic life and conduct with **God**—they nevertheless became lustful for strange foods and the meat-pots of Egypt. Therefore, **God** took no pleasure in most of them, for they were struck down in the wilderness.

What shall we say of our Israelites today? Has the heavenly food and the bread of the mighty—I mean the good Word of **God** and the powers of the world to come—ever fallen upon the dry and barren deserts of many thousands of hearts with such an abundant wealth of grace in many hundreds of years as in these last days? Has not everything lain white as snow, primarily outside the army camp of external Christianity, or the barriers of so-called *orthodoxy*? This is so that everyone who first [leaves] himself and his own flesh and blood, and thereafter also the campsites and pens—I mean the sects and natural religions of external Christianity—a

...left little behind, and could gather a whole heart full for their satisfaction and refreshment. And although a form of the Communion of the body and blood of our **Lord** was and is still held here and there, it mostly occurred only to reawaken such sensual sensations and sweetness in weak and dying dispositions, and to renew some outward fellowship of love among one another. Therefore, such exercises of the breaking of bread and the **love-feasts** <note>original: *Liebes-mähler*, referring to the early Christian 'Agape' meals.</note> brought forth all sorts of powers of false and impure love in souls that were not yet "dead to the world" and were inclined toward emotional sensations. When one thought that this or that person was quite fiery and glowing in true brotherly love—ready to leave not only property and goods, but body and blood for the **Lord** and the brothers—it often lasted no longer than such exercises and acts <note>original: *actus*</note> continued; afterwards, such poor hearts fell into even heavier temptations, deviations, offenses, doubts, and unbelief. ### What exactly is this threefold wilderness?

The denial and mortification of the **flesh** / the seclusion of the **soul** / and the **poverty of the spirit**.

Concerning the first wilderness or the state of denial of fleshly and earthly things / even though the Spirit of God's active love has enticed us to abstinence in a thousand ways / to the abandonment of fatherland / friendship / house and home / to physical poverty and separation from temporal goods / worldly honors / fleshly lusts and earthly comfort / drawn and driven [us] / **Reason** soon knew an excuse / how one must first set the heart free from them / otherwise nothing helps / if one remains stuck to them with one's desires / which is certainly true: Meanwhile, for many who were possessed by these things through the magic power of **self-love**, the heart would never become free; and if ever some chastening of the spirit unsettled the mind regarding these things / one did not want to trust such workings of God's grace so easily / and [to follow] after the infallible example...

Regarding the soul, indeed, we had all kinds of sweetness and grace from the **Lord** to enjoy in abundance: but only so that we, as poor weak children, might be enticed little by little by these gifts of love to seek the source and origin, our heavenly Father, from whom every good gift and every perfect present comes. ### What was the occasion for idolatry?

Regarding the occasion for the aforementioned grave sin of <term>idolatry</term> <note>German: *Abgötterey*. While this refers historically to the worship of the Golden Calf, the author uses it here to describe placing anything—including one's own desires—above God.</note>, we have heard above how the **Lord**, according to the counsel of His wisdom, chose the people of **Israel** from among all the nations and tribes of the earth to be His own special people. He called them through His faithful servant **Moses** out of **Egypt**, where they were oppressed in heavy servitude with hard labor, into His fellowship in the wilderness, so that He might sanctify and purify them there into a <term>royal priesthood</term> <note>A reference to Exodus 19:6 and 1 Peter 2:9, implying a community dedicated entirely to divine service.</note>, to offer up living sacrifices, both of reconciliation and peace with **God**, as well as the perfect thank-offerings of pure love and the praise of **God**.

Indeed, everything the **Lord** has shown this people until now—leading them out of **Egypt** with such a mighty hand, baptizing them in the <term>pillar of cloud</term> <note>German: *Wolcken-säule*. According to the Book of Exodus, God guided the Israelites by a visible pillar of cloud by day and fire by night.</note> by day and in the pillar of fire by night, and even in the Red Sea, feeding them with bread from heaven and giving them water to drink from the rock that followed them—all of this, I say, this people should have used for their sanctification and purification. It was intended to help them serve the **Lord** and to keep the great festival of reconciliation for 40 days and 40 nights in the wilderness, so that they might thus enter into the eternal rest of **Canaan** and into the land or the **Sabbath** <note>The author equates the "Promised Land" of Canaan with a spiritual state of "Sabbath" or eternal rest in God.</note> which **God** promised to their **fathers** with an oath. Let us now, **dear brothers!** with an impartial and unfeigned eye consider how we have actually stepped into all the footsteps of <term>apostasy|Abfalls; referring here to a spiritual falling away or rebellion against God</term> and disobedience, and have carried out in deed exactly what those people performed as a <note>The author views the Old Testament stories as "types" or "previews" (Vorbilder) of the spiritual life of the Christian.</note> symbolic preview. We shall see that the text of Moses requires no explanation: if only each person searches their own heart without self-deception, their own sorrowful experience will sufficiently explain this great <term>mystery of wickedness|Geheimniß der Boßheit; a reference to 2 Thessalonians 2:7, "the mystery of iniquity"</term> within them, to their own shame before **GOD**.

Regarding the first occasion of this, from the beginning of the <term>Awakening|Erweckung; a period of intense religious revival, specifically the Pietist movement in German-speaking lands</term>, people did not only confess orally, but also in many large writings before so-called spiritual and secular <term>commissions|Commissionen; official investigative committees or trials used by state churches to interrogate religious radicals</term>, examinations, and courts—yes, before all people—freely and open-heartedly. They urged one another to go out from the **land of Egypt**, where our dear **Lord** is crucified; for so one rightly called the kingdom of this world, and within it, external, <term>fallen Christianity|verfallene Christenthum; the belief that the institutional church had lost its primitive purity and spiritual power</term>. 

People exhorted one another to depart from all self-love, from temporal goods, worldly honors, and from everything that might hinder the soul from the intended journey of the true **following of Jesus**. They sought to follow the <note>original: "Hertzogen unserer Seeligkeit," a reference to the "Captain" or "Author" of salvation from Hebrews 2:10.</note> Prince of our salvation three days' journey into the **wilderness**—that is, into total <term>detachment|Abgeschiedenheit; the mystical practice of withdrawing one's desires and attention from all created things to focus solely on God</term> from all creatures, according to body, soul, and spirit. This was to be achieved through the way of purification, self-denial, and sanctification, leading to enlightenment and union with **GOD** through the <term>triple economy|dreyfache œconomie; the historical and spiritual stages through which the Father, Son, and Holy Spirit work to restore humanity</term> and revelation of the workings of the **Father, Son, and Holy Spirit**. With all their heart, all their soul, all their mind, and all their strength, they were to surrender themselves to **GOD** as an eternal offering of love, praise, and thanks. After the denial and death of the "old man," they were to walk with **GOD** in a new life as **GOD’s** own special people, and celebrate an eternal **Sabbath** in the **Land of Promise**. ...to conquer and mortify themselves in their **passions**. <note>original: "Affecten"; referring to the uncontrolled emotional and physical impulses that Pietists sought to discipline.</note> Some were very busy, attempting to bring the whole work of redemption to completion within themselves and others with great haste and force; this was commonly done through self-chosen works of a presumed **divine service**. Nevertheless, He still acts as our faithful **Mediator** with **God** His heavenly Father, offering powerful intercession that He might speak the living word of His own self into our hearts for the **new birth**, and through that, bring the work of **redemption** to completion.

The Spirit of **Wisdom**, through her hidden pull, also confirms these from within: that we should stand before **God** in struggle and wrestling, to put to death our active nature. To say little of it / this doctrine of the great **glory of Christ** on this earth was soon accepted by the earthly-minded / and it was easily believed by a power-hungry and headstrong nature that the **firstborn** and **chosen**—among whom were soon counted all those who gave even slight approval to this teaching—should reign with Christ for a thousand years on this earth. <note>This refers to "Chiliasm" or Millennialism, a belief popular in certain 17th-century radical circles that Christ would return to establish a literal 1,000-year kingdom on earth.</note> Thus, a great multitude of people expected it according to the image presented to them; to say nothing of the specific time / circumstances of the location / and the manner and form / of how and where it should begin.

...the way <note>The catchword from the previous page "den" completes the phrase "den Weg" (the way).</note> has not been found: From the outside, we find no more edification and blessing in any external **Divine Service**; from within, we are no better than before/ and what is justly suspicious/ we see instead more shame/ abominations, and scandals among us than in any external religion or sect: There is no order and wise governance; there is no reverence/ love, and discipline; everyone lives/ speaks/ runs and rushes about in confusion in the highest power of their own will. ## What do the people do now in such a condition?

In such a state of confusion and impatience the people gather against Aaron <note>original: "Aharon"; a reference to Exodus 32, where the Israelites, impatient for Moses’ return from Mount Sinai, pressured Aaron to create a physical idol (the Golden Calf).</note>. Has he not rather become a prototype of the Antichrist and the false prophet?

# What did Aaron do next?

He had a general festival proclaimed and announced: The Festival of the Lord <note>original: "Fest des HErrn"</note>, or in the Lord, is tomorrow. Without doubt, he is pointing to that which he spoke there in the name of the Lord to Pharaoh: *Thus says the Lord, the God of Israel: Let my people go, that they may hold a festival for me in the wilderness!* <note>Exodus 5:1</note> Likewise: *The God of the Hebrews has called us; so let us now go three days' journey into the wilderness, and sacrifice to the Lord our God!* <note>Exodus 3:18</note> About which we have already mentioned above, how God called the people of Israel into the wilderness and led them there as a trial, in order—by way of the renunciation of the world, flesh, and blood; through the emptying of the soul of all selfhood <term>Eigenheit: a key Pietist term for the selfish ego or "self-will" that must be abandoned to reach God</term>; and through poverty of spirit—to reach again that living communion with God from which Adam departed and fell in the Garden of Pleasure <note>Eden; the author uses "Wollust" (pleasure/delight) to contrast with the "Wüsten" (wilderness)</note>, which stands in contrast to the wilderness.

Now in the wilderness, after they had practiced well in the aforementioned renunciation through this three-fold day's journey, they were to hold a festival to *Jehovah* <note>The Hebrew name for God, used here to signify His eternal essence</note>; that is: once they had died to all their own activity and busyness, and had rested <term>gefeyret: from the German 'feiern,' meaning both to celebrate and to cease from labor, specifically referring to the Sabbath rest</term> from all their own works—both of actual sins and of self-chosen divine service—and thus all the attributes of God were revealed in them, up to the highest perfection and rest in the name *Jehovah*, they would then have come into that state of holding the great festival of reconciliation and union with *Jehovah*, the Ground, Primal State, and Essence of the highest perfection; and so then, through this union, they would have entered into that rest and Sabbath with God which the Lord has prepared for His people. The poor blind people believed nothing else than that they were already in the state to hold this great festival; and Aaron himself believed that, because his molten calf corresponded so perfectly with everyone’s *idea* <note>original Latin term for a mental concept or internal image</note> and opinion, God had [revealed] himself to them

<summary>The author interprets the Golden Calf festival as a false, superficial imitation of the true spiritual rest and union with God intended for Israel in the wilderness. He argues that true worship requires an internal "emptying" of the self rather than outward ceremonies or "self-chosen" rituals.</summary>

It contrasts "hand-made" temples and empty ceremonies with the internal, spiritual "living stones" of the true church. The text concludes by defining true preparation for God as self-denial rather than outward ritual.</meta>

<page-num>54</page-num>

...through this very same spirit of error, just as this father of ours <note>Likely referring to Aaron, who prefigured this error by facilitating the worship of the Golden Calf</note> prefigured for us. What a thousand speculations and reflections do we not make, about how we might entice, form, and mold one another into external worship <note>original: "Gottes-Dienst"</note>? What thousands of institutions, church ordinances, articles, religious customs, points, and dogmas are there not? Instead of joining ourselves together as living stones <note>A reference to 1 Peter 2:5, where believers are "living stones" built into a spiritual house</note> into a single house and temple of the Holy Spirit, where one should be built into the other, the best stones are rejected, and the others allow themselves to be built into the idol-temple of the false churches. 

Instead of setting up that altar from which those who serve the tabernacle have no right to eat <note>Hebrews 13:10</note>—both in temples made with hands and in one’s own external fleshly and animal-man <note>original: "Thier-Menschen"; referring to the "natural man" who lacks spiritual rebirth</note>—instead, one person compels and drives the other into the fleshly tabernacle- and temple-service of empty ceremonies, to the fellowship of that altar from which even those are permitted to eat who are drunk with the wine of the wrath of Babylon’s fornication <note>A common Radical Pietist metaphor for the corrupt state churches, drawn from Revelation 17</note>, and with the blood and vengeance against the true children of God; to say nothing of other abominations of injustice and impurity with which these supposed sanctuaries are daily defiled and desecrated. 

These are the altars that people want to see erected and promoted in writing and by word of mouth. But whoever grasps the Lord in pure love will follow Him with joyful obedience wherever He goes, regardless of all contradiction—even outside the gates of the camp <note>Hebrews 13:13, referring to following Christ in his rejection by the established religious order</note>—and bear His reproach, eating His crucified body and drinking His shed blood upon His pure altar, proclaiming and magnifying His death through the mortification of self <term>Abtödtung: the spiritual practice of "dying" to one's own ego and desires to be unified with God</term>, until He comes. For true children of God know of no other preparation for the great feast of Jehovah <note>The Hebrew name for God, emphasizing His eternal and holy nature</note> than an honest and faithful following, in self-denial and mortification of self, in reproach and shame, through the cross and suffering, even unto the loss of their souls.

<summary>The author denounces institutional church rituals as "idol-temples" and "empty ceremonies," urging believers to instead become "living stones" in a spiritual temple. True worship is described as following Christ "outside the camp" through the internal mortification of the self.</summary>

# What did Aaron do next?

He had a general festival proclaimed and announced: The Festival of the Lord, or in the Lord, is tomorrow. Without a doubt, he was pointing to that which he had spoken there to Pharaoh in the name of the Lord: Thus says the Lord, the God of Israel: Let my people go, that they may hold a feast to me in the wilderness! Likewise: The God of the Hebrews has called us; so let us now go a three days' journey into the wilderness, and sacrifice to the Lord our God! Regarding this, we have already mentioned above how God called the people of Israel into the wilderness and led them there as a test, in order to reach living communion with God again—from which Adam departed and fell in the Garden of Pleasure <note>original: "Garten der Wollust"; referring to Eden</note>, which is set in opposition to the wilderness—through the way of renouncing the world, flesh, and blood; through the emptying of the soul of all selfhood <note>original: "Eigenheit"; a mystical term for the ego or self-will that separates a person from God</note>; and through poverty of spirit. 

In the wilderness now, after they had practiced well in the aforementioned renunciation through this triple day's journey, they were to hold a feast to Jehovah; that is: when they had died to all their own activity and busyness, and had rested from all their own works—both from actual sins and from self-chosen worship <note>original: "selbst-erwählten GOttes-Diensts"</note>—and thus all the attributes of God were revealed in them, even unto the highest perfection and rest in the name of Jehovah, they would then have come into that state of holding the great Feast of Reconciliation and Union with Jehovah, the Ground, Primal State, and Essence of the highest perfection; and so, through this union, they would have entered into that rest and Sabbath with God which the Lord has prepared for His people. 

The poor, blind people thought nothing else than that they were in the state to hold this great feast; and Aaron himself believed—because his cast calf so perfectly matched everyone’s idea <note>original: "idea"</note> and opinion—that God had [revealed himself] to them...

It argues that true worship requires an internal "emptying" of the self to achieve union with God, rather than the external ceremonies symbolized by the Golden Calf.</summary>

...be glorified and revealed in His most high Name.

But what do we do, dear brothers? We all know that we are called out of Egypt, through the wilderness, and into rest; to go out of our flesh and blood, through the cross and suffering, into the Kingdom of God. The way of self-denial <note>original: "Verláugnung"</note> of both body and soul, and the poverty of spirit, are the only means to arrive at true communion with God. The Prince and Leader of our salvation <note>referring to Jesus, based on Hebrews 2:10</note> preceded us by no other way than this.

Now, one person may take up self-denial in an external way, and another in an internal way; yet everyone is called to the mortification <note>original: "Abtódtung"; the spiritual practice of "killing off" the selfish nature</note> of themselves and of self-love, whether it be on the outside, on the inside, or in all places. The explanations and opinions of others will not accomplish this; everyone must take hold of themselves and, during this time in the wilderness, bring home into themselves the working power of the renunciation, suffering, and death of Jesus. One must learn to rest <note>original: "feyren"; to keep a Sabbath or "cease" from labor</note> from all one's own life, activities, and works—whether they be physical or spiritual—so that Jehovah may be glorified in us, and we may hold the glorious feast of reconciliation through Christ in and with Him.

Even though we were still far removed from the state of this complete detachment <note>original: "Abgeschiedenheit"; a mystical term for being "set apart" from worldly desires to focus on God</note>, and had taken barely three steps (let alone three days' journey) into the wilderness—indeed, having not even stepped upon the true ground and battlefield of genuine self-denial and the proper test—matters were nevertheless measured out. This was done not according to our ability or our weak condition, but according to the <term>astral time: a concept in radical mysticism where events are dictated by cosmic or astrological cycles rather than inner spiritual growth</term>. According to this "astral" timing, we certainly could have been masters, though we had not even become true disciples.

These times were called a Day of Preparation, a Pre-Sabbath, and the true <term>Philadelphia: the "Church of Brotherly Love" mentioned in the Book of Revelation, which many 18th-century Pietists believed represented the final, pure state of the church</term>, where one should prepare for the approaching great feast and await with joy the glorious revelation or coming of the Lord. Instead, however, of turning our desires away from all our own activity <note>the original German breaks here with "Würck-" and the catchword "samkeit," forming "Würcksamkeit"</note> during this time...

He critiques his community for claiming spiritual maturity based on "astral time" before they had actually undergone the necessary inner trials.</summary>

[watchful]ness / to free our understanding from all those false images and forms / and to let our dear Lord form, govern, and prepare us Himself through His Holy Spirit; instead, we were occupied with a thousand kinds of arrangements and well-intentioned preparations, through which we became more distorted / and truly unfit / to receive our Lord. ### How did Aaron celebrate this feast?

The text says: They rose early in the morning / and saw to it / that whole burnt offerings <term>Brand-Opffer: sacrifices where the entire animal was consumed by fire, symbolizing total devotion to God</term> ascended / and they brought forth peace offerings <term>Friedens-Opffer: communal sacrifices followed by a meal, signifying harmony between God and the people</term>; and the people sat down to eat / and to drink / and rose up to play <note>This is a direct reference to Exodus 32:6, describing the Israelites' behavior while worshipping the Golden Calf</note>. That is how it goes! Those who are of the flesh / their minds are set on the things of the flesh. This people was fleshly and animal-minded / so they made themselves an animal god: and as they had an animal god / so they also served him according to an animalistic sense and with the works of the flesh. We do not even need to think back so far / to the Israel of the flesh / whom God led out of Egypt into the wilderness for this very reason / so that the lustful beast of the evil nature in their flesh and blood might be mortified <note>original: "abgetödtet"; a central pietist concept of "killing off" the ego and sinful desires</note> / and the true service of the Spirit in faith and obedience might be revealed in them. Therefore He let them pass through the Red Sea; therefore He gave them His food from heaven; therefore He led them to Mount Horeb / and had a fence made around the mountain / so that neither beast nor man should touch the mountain / lest they be killed <note>Reference to Exodus 19:12-13, where God sets boundaries for His holiness</note> / to indicate / that man may not draw near into communion with God / before he has killed off his animal nature / and [entered] into true sanctification

<meta>This page continues the thought from page 57. The fragment "gung" at the start of this page likely completes the word "Vereinigung" (union), as the author discusses how the people sought a way to reach union with God through their own carnal methods rather than through the Spirit.</meta>

[union] is reached; just as the Word spoken from the mountain <note>A reference to God speaking the Ten Commandments from Mount Sinai in Exodus 20</note> produced nothing but the agony of death, fear, and terror in the people because of their carnal mind, so that according to their fleshly inclination they preferred to speak with their visible leader <note>Moses</note> rather than with God. For this reason, God took this leader of theirs out of their carnal sight and into the darkness with Him, so that they might learn to grasp God in "dark faith" <note>A mystical term for faith that does not rely on physical sight or external evidence</note> and to recognize and honor their invisible, only true Leader. He left them for forty days in the parched wilderness without any external presence of a commander, nor any external, carnal worship—neither sacrifices nor burnt offerings, nor any sensory sanctuary—so that they might learn to worship the only true God in the manner He is worthy of: to be worshiped in Spirit and in Truth.

But they remained constant and immovable in their carnal mind; and so that they might keep their flesh and blood untainted and yet still attain communion with a God, they made for themselves a GOD according to their own animalistic likeness. And whereas they should have prepared themselves as living sacrifices for the living GOD, they instead sacrificed mere oxen and calves to their calf-god. They served this beastly image according to beastly desires with eating and drinking; and when they had filled their bellies, they rose up to play and danced like wanton calves around the calf. And as they were driven to and fro like slaves by the fire of nature in the ropes and bonds of flesh and blood, they raised a great shout of jubilee and cheered one another on in the dance with loud triumphs. Thus, the entire, otherwise well-ordered army of Israel became a raging and frantic *mob* <note>original: "Turba"; a Latin term often used in Jacob Boehme’s philosophy to describe a state of wrathful disorder, confusion, or a breakdown of divine harmony</note>, driven in a circle around the calf by carnal religious zeal, so that they could be restrained by neither external nor internal discipline.

It is we / who not only surrender ourselves along with all our powers upon these self-made altars / for service and sacrifice / but even compel, entice, drive, and push others into the fellowship of our own altars! Then, before long, each among us stretches out his hand / to lead a round dance / and shouts at the top of his lungs / to see if someone will dance to his voice and pipe <note>original: "nach seiner Stimme und Pfeiffen tantzen"; a common idiom meaning to follow someone's lead or obey them blindly.</note> / and allow themselves to be driven around in the circle of his own invention. Understand me well! I do not only mean those who call us back to the general circles of religions for a dance with a loud outcry / and pull us there with outstretched arms / so that the round dances are not destroyed / and the circles are not thrown into disorder / so that everyone in their religion, <term>sect</term>, and order / in which they were born and raised / might dance around to the melody / that has been piped to them since youth: But—

<meta>The text on this page continues the thought from the previous page. ...but rather, I also mean those among us who, while they do not follow the general rule of the large religious circles into the coarse and desolate nature of idolatry, nor do they respond to the common musicians of those learned only in the letter <note>The "letter" refers to a literal, intellectual understanding of the Bible without spiritual life, as in "the letter kills, but the Spirit gives life" (2 Corinthians 3:6).</note> with verbal approval or active imitation. Instead, they have made a GOD according to their own discretion and form. They have decorated and adorned their own opinions with the images of Scripture and the forms of ancient Christianity, yet inside, it is nothing but a dead and empty image. They have chosen a specific service for this image and built an altar with nothing but self-devised statutes—an altar from which no one has the power to eat except those who, according to these established rules, allow themselves to be driven around in their sealed circle, closed off by covenant and ban.

And even if one were to offer a hand with good and correct instructions and try to lead someone out of this raging <term>Turba: A term from the mystic Jakob Böhme, referring to a state of chaotic turmoil, wrath, or the "uproar" of the fallen world.</term> onto the inner, living path of truth, such exhortations are only understood figuratively by distorted minds. The most simple teachings are turned into nothing but powerless forms and empty opinions.

Therefore, let each of us reach into our own breast and examine ourselves. Then we will find the <term>Archetype: original: "Archetypum"; the fundamental pattern or original model from which copies are made.</term> and basic pattern—in which all the golden calves of our own images and godly forms are cast and shaped—within our own reason and imagination. For everyone makes themselves into a golden calf and a beloved idol. And if no one will sacrifice to them, eat, drink, or dance to their tune according to their own whim, then they run around in circles centered on themselves, driven by the impulse of their own natural fire, even if they were sitting in the loneliest wilderness or private chamber. For because we have fallen into a beastly addiction to images out of base self-love, we are [driven] by such a driving spirit of vertigo <note>original: "Schwindel-Geist"; literally a "dizzy-spirit," referring to a state of spiritual confusion or delusion that makes the soul spin in circles.</note>...

<summary>The author warns that even those who reject organized religion can fall into "internal idolatry" by creating a god based on their own opinions and ego. He argues that the "Archetype" of all idols is found within human reason and self-love, leading to spiritual confusion.</summary>

...is tried, and they swarm about in confusion until we become weary and tired of it. Then we think—because the golden calves of our own-made images do not satisfy our souls, nor are they able to lead us into the true rest in Canaan <note>In this mystical context, Canaan represents a state of spiritual peace and the presence of God, rather than just a physical land.</note>—that there is no living God. We conclude that all true worship of the saints, from the beginning until now, is a pure human invention, fantasy, and opinion used to deceive people into outward obedience. In this most dangerous temptation, many thousands of clever **slaves to reason** <term>slaves to reason: original: "Vernunfft-Sclaven"; those who are bound by their own intellectual logic and refuse to acknowledge spiritual experiences that transcend the mind.</term> are captured and led into eternal destruction.

### What then followed the Israelite calf-worship?

And **Jehovah** spoke to **Moses**: "Go, descend! For the people whom you caused to come up out of the land of **Egypt** have corrupted themselves." Beloved! In what way then have the people corrupted themselves? Through this terrible sin of idolatry and spiritual adultery, the people have corrupted themselves and so recklessly desecrated their virginity <note>The author uses "virginity" as a metaphor for the soul's pure, original state and its exclusive devotion to God.</note> that they could not grasp the seed of God in the living words which Moses was to bring down from the mountain and instill within them. Thus, they could not attain the true **rebirth** through spirit and truth, because they had distorted themselves with animal-like imagery and shamefully defiled themselves with the impure heat of fleshly desires.

Therefore, Moses, their mediator, could no longer stand before the pure eyes of the Lord, but had to descend—that is, he had to turn back from the fellowship with **Jehovah**, in which he stood on behalf of the people and received the powers for the new birth. This was because everything he had done until then, and was doing even now in the name of this people, would be useless and in vain. For they have quickly turned aside from the way I commanded them; they have made a cast calf within themselves, and have bowed down to it, and have sacrificed to it.

<summary>The author argues that internal idolatry and reliance on human reason lead to spiritual despair and a rejection of God, paralleling the Israelites' corruption with the golden calf. This internal "defilement" prevents the soul from achieving spiritual rebirth and disrupts the role of the divine mediator.</summary>

...and said: "These are your gods, Israel! Who have caused you to ascend out of the land of **Egypt**." The Lord had shown them the true and proper way in the words He spoke from **Sinai**, so that they might reach the true, living knowledge and communion with God in the name **Jehovah**; for the word **Torah** <term>Torah: Usually translated as "Law," the author here emphasizes its etymological roots in the Hebrew word 'yarah' (to throw or point), defining it as a "direction" or "guidance."</term>, which is otherwise called "the Law," means a correct guidance, which one points out or casts before the eyes with the hand. The main foundation for wisdom and union with God is also contained in the first word: "I am **Jehovah**, your God, who led you out of **Egypt**, out of the house of bondage; you shall have no foreign gods before my face, and you shall not make for yourself any graven or carved image," etc.

What is more clear, then, than that God wanted to test them by this trial to make them free from all foreign gods and empty of all preconceived ideas and images in the wilderness? He did this so that He might become manifest to them according to all His attributes, even in the name **Jehovah**, and become known through living experience. From this true and only way they have deviated with great haste and in a short time, and have fallen into such a dangerous and terrible error that all their previous pilgrimage and the departure through the Red Sea into the wilderness—along with everything they themselves suffered and endured—may be of no help.

And **Jehovah** spoke to **Moses**: "I have observed this people, and behold, it is a stiff-necked people; they who always turn their back to the face of the Lord, so that even when He wishes to be glorified and revealed in them through His grace, mercy, and wisdom, they still will not allow themselves to be bowed or turned so that they might learn to know Him face to face." <note>The "back" versus the "face" is a common mystical motif representing the difference between seeing God's shadow/works and experiencing His direct presence.</note>

...can reason prevent the almighty **God** from consuming them, when He says: "Let me be!"? It is not my intention to delve into an extensive explanation of the text of **Moses**; nevertheless, since it might serve our purpose of brotherly instruction and exhortation, we wish to show how one might remove the veil from **Moses**'s face <note>A reference to Exodus 34:33-35. Here, the "veil" represents the literal or superficial understanding of Scripture that hides a deeper spiritual truth.</note> and understand the imagery when Scripture speaks of **God**'s wrath, hatred, enmity, love, joy, and similar human passions <term>Affecten: from the Latin 'affectus,' referring to emotions or passions that move the soul; the author argues these are human projections onto the divine.</term>; inasmuch as the poor, misguided human, through such ways of speaking, forms an image of such a **God** in his imagination that resembles a loathsome beast-man, full of coarse passions and emotions.

Understand now: when **God** created man in the beginning in His image, his eye was so simple <note>In the sense of "undivided" or "single-minded" (from the Latin *simplex*).</note> that he knew his **God** in no other way than in the highest simplicity and as a single attribute. God was manifest to him in and according to the unique name **Jehovah**, and man was ignorant of what love or wrath, mercy or justice, this or that might be in **God**. Because man was simple and had only one single desire, **God** was also to him the only thing that he wanted and craved.

But as soon as man turned his will away from **God** through lust and directed his desire toward something foreign outside of **God**, man also—through eating and grasping that which he sought and hungered for outside of **God**—became foreign in essence and contrary to the nature of **God**. Thus, although the one and unchangeable **Jehovah** constantly worked within man in one and the same attribute <term>Einwürckung: Divine "in-working" or influence; the internal operation of God's presence in the soul.</term>, man nonetheless perceived this single influence as entirely foreign and hostile. Because man felt sorrow, anxiety, and adversity within himself, he sought to resist **God**'s influence through his own activity, or to grasp it according to his own cravings. 

But because man—indeed, even **Lucifer** himself—cannot live, move, or exist <note>original: "leben/ weben noch seyn," a common German rendering of Acts 17:28, "In him we live and move and have our being."</note> without this divine influence, and yet resists it out of great anxiety and torment...

<summary>The author explains that "wrath" and "hatred" are not attributes of God, but rather human distortions caused by the Fall. When humans turned away from God's simple essence (Jehovah), they began to experience His unchanging presence as something painful or hostile.</summary>

Names / to be seen in the 2nd Book of Moses <note>The 2nd Book of Moses is the Book of Exodus.</note> XXXIII, 18, 19; Chapter XXXIV, 5, 6, 7, 8; of which perhaps more [will be said] below. But since the people, through alienation from **GOD** and their own workings, had fallen back into the first ground and state of nature—namely, into wrath—the influence of **GOD**'s attribute caused anguish <note>original: "Angst"</note> within their hearts that were working against **GOD**. In this state, the terrifying wrath of **GOD** was ignited all the more because they had already come somewhat closer to **GOD** than they had been by nature. Consequently, Moses could not break through further on the mountain on behalf of the people. And because **GOD** had already worked out and brought forth the attributes of His mercy, grace, and goodness in Moses, the wrath of **GOD** could not fully overcome him. Therefore, the singular attribute <note>The author refers to God’s nature being one, but appearing differently to the soul depending on its state.</note> revealed itself in Moses as a conflict; the working of **GOD**, which ignited as wrath in Moses’s nature, said: *Let me be, I will consume [them]!*

Thus, the Word worked wrath in Moses’s [lower] nature, but grace in his new birth, and it became a mighty conflict within him. And in such an inner conflict, Moses entered into anguish; and in that anguish, the wrath of **GOD** tested Moses to see how strong he had become in his love and faithfulness toward the people: *I will make or work you into a great nation!* That is: everything **GOD** has worked and done in your soul shall be for you and your descendants.

Names / to be seen in the Second Book of Moses <note>The book of Exodus.</note> XXXIII, chapters 18, 19, and XXXIV, 5, 6, 7, 8, about which perhaps more will be said below. Therefore, the singular attribute <note>The author argues that God has only one nature, but it manifests as either love or wrath depending on the recipient's spiritual state.</note> revealed itself in Moses as a conflict; the operation of **God**, which ignited as wrath in Moses's nature, said: 

> **"Let me alone, that I may consume [them]!"**

> **"Why should your wrath burn hot against your people, which you have brought forth out of the land of Egypt with great power and with a mighty hand? Why should the Egyptians speak, saying: 'He brought them out for mischief, to kill them'..."**

<meta>This page continues the theological analysis of Moses' intercession on Mount Sinai. While the previous page described the internal struggle within Moses between the "birth of love" and the "fire of wrath," this page provides the specific scriptural dialogue and explains the mystical meaning of "God's repentance."</meta>

...slay [them] in the mountains and make an end of them from the face of the **Adamah**? <note>original: "Adamah," the Hebrew word for ground or earth.</note> Turn back from the burning of your nose <note>original: "Brand deiner Nasen," a literal translation of the Hebrew idiom for "fierce anger."</note> and be merciful because of the wickedness of your people! Remember **Abraham**, **Isaac**, and **Israel**, your servants, to whom you swore by yourself and said to them: "I will multiply your seed like the stars of the heavens, and all this earth of which I have spoken I will give to their seed, and they shall possess it forever!" And **Jehovah** repented of the evil which he had spoken to do to his people, and he turned. From these words, it can be sufficiently seen what a mighty struggle was in **Moses**, where the birth of love wrestled so long with the breaking-in power of wrath in his inner self until the former overcame the latter. And since the conflict was mighty within him, he also seized the Word, which had been formed and born in the fathers as the promise of blessing and peace; so that finally the singular attribute <note>The author posits that God's nature is a unified whole, but humans experience it as different "attributes" like wrath or love based on their own spiritual state.</note> was revealed in him no longer in wrath, but in grace and love. This is what occurs when the wrath of **God** in man is transformed into love, and the anguish and conflict cease—this is called in Scripture the "repentance of **God**." This change is not in **God**—as reason imagines a **God** outside of itself beyond the stars—but rather repentance forms within man himself, when the working of the living Word in the new birth of love gains the upper hand over the birth of wrath, or nature.

It is remarkable what is stated in the following verse, yet belongs inseparably here: **And He turned.** **Luther** attributes these words to **Moses** and translates: "And Moses turned." Yet not only does "**Moses**" not appear before it in the original text, but **Stephen**, the blood-witness <term>blood-witness: translated from 'Blut-Zeug,' a traditional term for a martyr who testifies to their faith through death</term> of **Jesus**, himself attributes these words to **God** in his testimony concerning **Israel**, saying: "And they made a calf..."

<summary>This page explains that "God's repentance" is actually a transformation within the human soul where love overcomes wrath, using Moses' intercession as the primary example. The author also disputes Luther's translation of Exodus, arguing that it was God, not Moses, who "turned" in response to the intercession.</summary>

<keywords>Moses, intercession, wrath of God, repentance of God, divine attributes, Martin Luther, Saint Stephen, new birth, internal struggle, Hebrew idioms</keywords>
