<meta>This page continues the theological reflection from page 72 regarding Moses' descent from Mount Sinai. While the previous page established that Moses had to "step back" spiritually due to the people's sins, this page interprets the "tablets of testimony" he carried not as physical objects, but as a metaphor for his own spiritually transformed being.</meta>

What else were the two tablets of the testimony but the understanding and the heart of **Moses**, which **God** had thoroughly engraved through the constant influence of His Word? It was done in such a way that, from whichever side or passage one looked at them, one could recognize the true <term>image of God</term> <note>original: *Ebenbild GOttes*. A reference to the *Imago Dei*, the theological concept of humanity being created in God's likeness, here fully restored in Moses.</note> according to all the powers of the divine mind. One could look and pass through into this perfect, royal <term>law of liberty</term> <note>original: *Gesetz der Freyheit*. A phrase found in the Epistle of James (1:25), here used to contrast the internal spiritual law with external legalism.</note> because **God**, together with all His <term>attributes</term> <note>original: *Eigenschafften*. Refers to the specific characteristics or qualities of God's nature, such as love, justice, or holiness.</note>, had so <term>essentially</term> <note>original: *wesentlich*. In this context, it means "pertaining to the inner essence or being," suggesting a literal transformation of Moses' soul rather than a mere symbolic one.</note> <term>transfigured</term> <note>original: *verkläret*. This refers to a spiritual state where the divine glory becomes manifest within a person, similar to the Transfiguration of Jesus.</note> and revealed Himself within them. No dark shadows or images were reflected any longer, nor could anything hinder the passage into the knowledge and the will of **God**. 

Thus, these were not material tablets; rather, it is to be understood as the essential work of sanctification, enlightenment, and union with **God** within the mind, understanding, and heart of **Moses**. Therefore the text says: **God** Himself made them, and He Himself engraved that which was etched into them. For this was not the old, but the living <term>new covenant</term> <note>original: *neue Bund*. The author is arguing that Moses experienced the "New Covenant" (internal law) internally, even while the "Old Covenant" (external law) was being given to the people.</note> which the **Lord** sought to establish in **Moses** with all **Israel** through the transfiguration of Himself, and which He later promised to establish anew:

> I will put my law in their heart, and write it in their understanding, and I will be their **God**, and they shall be my people; and no one shall teach his neighbor, and no one his brother, saying: Know the **Lord**! because they shall all know me, from the least among them to the greatest among them. Jeremiah 31, Hebrews 8.

**Moses** had these tablets in his hand, not just before his eyes, so that he might have perceived and recognized this mystery; rather, he had them in his power, in his possession and enjoyment. Thus, when he stepped among the children of **Israel** in such clarity and glory, they could have recognized in **Moses** the true image of **God** and **Jesus Christ** in the new humanity and birth, and the

...might be able to behold the **glory of the Lord** with an uncovered face as in a mirror, and be transformed into that same image from clarity to clarity <note>The author refers to 2 Corinthians 3:18. The concept of the *Imago Dei*, which the author believes was perfectly restored in Moses during his time on the mountain.</note>.

But as **Moses** approached the camp and saw the calf and the dancing, his "nose"—or rather his anger—burned hot <note>The author is playing on a Hebrew idiom where "the nose burning" means "to become angry." He provides a mystical interpretation of this physiological reaction.</note>. That is to say: all the forces of **God's** inward working and the <term>attribute</term> <note>original: *Eigenschafft*. A term likely influenced by Jacob Boehme, referring to the specific qualities or "temperaments" of the divine nature.</note> or the **Living Word**—which had ignited **Moses** toward true union in the pure fire of **God’s** love, so that even his "nose" exhaled and breathed out the fragrance of the fervent **Love of Jesus**—now worked as anger and wrath within the turned-away **Moses** as he descended the mountain. This occurred such that the smoke and fire ignited and choked his "nose"—that is, the vessel of his life-spirit and breath. For when he came down to the bottom of the mountain—that is, when he had retreated back through all the <term>degrees</term> <note>original: *gradus*. Refers to the stages of spiritual ascent toward God or descent back into the material world.</note> from the communion of high union and peace with **God**, and had returned to the camp and to the <term>first principle</term> <note>original: *erste principium*. In mystical theology, this often refers to the realm of nature or the "fire-world" apart from light.</note>, or to the people in their state of nature—he threw the tablets from his hand and broke them. 

This means: he could not perfectly bring back and reveal the pure image of **God** (which had been renewed within him during his union with **God**) to the disobedient people within himself; for it was broken and shattered into pieces by the anger within him. Therefore, from that time on, the people could not enter into the perfect law of liberty and love except in a <term>piecemeal</term> <note>original: *Stück-weise*. Meaning fragmented or incomplete knowledge.</note> fashion. This is why it is said: *For we know in part, and we prophesy in part. But when that which is perfect is come, then that which is in part shall be done away.* **1 Corinthians 13:9–10.**

<meta>This page continues the discussion of Moses descending the mountain. Having previously described Moses' internal transformation and his holy anger, the text now transitions to his specific actions regarding the golden calf and the spiritual "grinding down" of the Israelites' idolatry.</meta>

...over the face of the waters and gave it to the children of Israel to drink.

The literal scholars <note>original: *Buchstab-Gelehrten*. Refers to those who interpret scripture only by its outward, "dead" letter rather than its inward spiritual meaning.</note> may understand these words in crude images and figures; however, they will never reach the true meaning of them in that way. We also allow the <term>Chemists</term> <note>original: *Chymicos*. In this era, alchemy and chemistry were not yet distinct; the author refers here to alchemists.</note> or laboratory artists to critique and speculate upon this, suggesting how Moses was a true <term>Adept</term> <note>original: *Adeptus*. In alchemy, one who has mastered the "Great Work" and attained the philosopher's stone.</note> who, by the help of the art, calcined the melted <term>Body</term> <note>original: *Corpus*</note> ☉ <note>The alchemical and astronomical symbol for Gold and the Sun.</note> of their golden calf into the subtlest powder and dust, dissolved it in <term>Royal Water</term> <note>original: *Aqua Regis*. A highly corrosive mixture of acids used to dissolve gold.</note>, soaked it with his <term>radical moisture</term> <note>original: *humido radicali*. In alchemy, the essential life-sustaining fluid or "soul" of a substance.</note>, and thereby tinted <note>original: *tingirt*. An alchemical term for transforming or "dyeing" a substance into a higher state of being.</note> all of Israel toward the new birth of the Paradisiacal Body. We do not wish to answer these people in their own dance, much less to dance around their gold-hungry calf.

It is proper for us to keep to that meaning which is bound to the whole testimony, and which can redeem and eternally bless our God-seeking souls from the error of idolatry and the service of all that is not God by nature. For since the golden calf was melted together from the lustful love-forces of all Israel, and all their hearts and desires were cast and embodied together into such a <term>Body</term> <note>original: *Corpus*</note> and image, no single heart or desire could be freed from it and brought back into the true Center, unless this golden calf were crushed again into as many parts and specks of dust as there were six hundred thousand people whose impure and idolatrous desires had melted it together. For everything that is composed in impurity and unrighteousness cannot be purified otherwise, unless it is not only crushed into many parts, but its body and composition dissolved in its innermost foundation. Thus Moses also brought, through the power of the living and inwardly powerful-working Word—which is sharper than any two-edged sword, and pierces until it divides soul and spirit <note>A reference to the biblical passage Hebrews 4:12, describing the power of the Word of God to judge the heart.</note>—

<summary>The author interprets Moses' destruction of the golden calf as a necessary spiritual act to dissolve the collective idolatry of the Israelites. He rejects a purely literal reading and critiques alchemical interpretations, arguing instead that the "Body" of the calf had to be utterly broken down to release the people's hearts from their false desires.</summary>

## But what did Moses say to Aaron?

<margin>v. 21.</margin> And Moses said to Aaron: *What did this people do to you, that you have brought such a great sin upon them?* <note>See Exodus 32:21.</note> From this we see clearly enough how Aaron and all his descendants—or those who stand in his office and in his spirit—are the greatest and most frequent cause of the blindness, idolatry, and coarse sins of the people. Whereas they should have introduced grace, mercy, reconciliation, and the forgiveness of sins into the people, they instead lead the poor people—with their sensual and external calf-worship <note>A reference to the Golden Calf; used here to criticize outward religious rituals that lack internal spiritual life.</note> and priest-craft <note>original: *Pfaffen-Kram*. A derogatory term for the rituals, traditions, and administrative business of the established clergy.</note>—into nothing but hardening of the heart, idolatry, ignorance, and all kinds of coarse vices. Afterward, however, they cast all the guilt upon the poor people, saying with Aaron: *You know this people, that they are set on evil!* <margin>v. 22.</margin> Instead of admitting their own guilt and, with true priestly faithfulness, taking the guilt of the idolatry and the punishment of the sin upon themselves to become an *anathema* <note>original: *Anathema*. A Greek term meaning someone or something "accursed." The author refers to the spiritual ideal of a leader being willing to be cursed or separated from God if it would save their people (as in Romans 9:3).</note> for them, it is said: "The people are stuck in evil; they will not let themselves be shown the way, but want to continue in their own self-will."

<term>Moses</term>: The biblical leader representing the Law and direct communication with God.
<term>Aaron</term>: The High Priest, here used as a symbol for established clergy who may fail their people.
<term>Reconciliation</term> (Versöhnung): The process of restoring the relationship between God and humanity.
<term>Idolatry</term> (Abgötterey): The worship of false gods or putting worldly things before the divine.
<term>Calf-worship</term> (Kälber-Dienst): Following empty, physical rituals instead of spiritual truth.
<term>Priest-craft</term> (Pfaffen-Kram): Derogatory term for clerical systems and ceremonies.
<term>Anathema</term>: A formal curse or state of being cast out.
<term>Self-will</term> (Eigensinn): Choosing one's own desires over God's direction.

<summary>This page concludes a prayer for spiritual purification and then moves into a sharp critique of religious leaders (represented by Aaron) who lead their followers into "calf-worship" and then blame the people for their own spiritual failings.</summary>
<keywords>High Priest, Jesus Christ, Moses, Aaron, Golden Calf, anti-clericalism, spiritual harlotry, reconciliation, priest-craft, Exodus 32</keywords>

--- Page 106 ---
<meta>This page continues the critique of the religious establishment, moving from Moses’ initial question to Aaron on the previous page to a detailed analysis of Aaron’s excuse and the clergy's role in misleading the people.</meta>

The local pastor is not to blame; he applies all diligence to direct the people away from all idolatry and *superstition* <note>original: *superstition*. In this period, writers often used Latinate loanwords to denote "false" or "catholic-style" religious practices.</note> toward the true living GOD and His service. Meanwhile, if someone misses even a single dance and does not diligently run along in the circle-dance and play along in proper form, exactly as it is sung and whistled to him, he is denounced by the Holy Priestly and Teaching Office as an erring spirit, disobedient and rebellious—not to mention that most of these servants of Aaron, out of shameful greed (which is itself a form of idolatry), run about day and night, shouting and crying out: "Whoever has gold or silver, let him tear it from his heart and give it to me!" Indeed, they take the very morsel from the mouths of the poor and enrich themselves from the sweat and blood of the oppressed. Despite this, there are still those advocates who help cast the primary cause of such horrors and exploitation upon the sins of the people and their own wickedness. We see also with what serpentine cunning Aaron excuses his abomination, saying: *And I threw it into the fire, and this calf came out*; he thus remains silent about how he himself first designed that "beautiful little beast" in such a shape and figure with a graving tool <term>graving tool: a stylus or chisel used for carving or engraving metal, referring here to the intellectual "shaping" of false doctrines</term>. Thus it still happens today: when the false priesthood has designed an idol along with its church service using a graving tool, and has diligently arranged, established, and formed everything according to their own self-will and opinion, they nevertheless want to persuade true servants of God that this was not their own invention, but that it was born and came out of the fire and the desire to serve God of its own accord—and that these people are now so accustomed to this form of worship that they cannot be turned away from it.

For since sin is actually nothing other than a turning away from **God** and the introduction of desire into something that is not **God**, nor according to His attributes, it happens that sin creates a stain and darkness in the soul. The soul is like a clarified mirror from which nothing but the pure **Image of God** should shine forth through His illumination and revelation; but sin prevents the pure majesty of the Godhead from presenting and manifesting itself. Although the sin of the people greatly troubled and distressed **Moses**, the stain and darkness were not innate in his soul, nor so effective that the entire **Image of God**, according to all the features <note>original: "lineamenten"</note> of Divine attributes, could have been erased in him.

<page-num>89</page-num>

From this testimony, it can be clearly recognized that **Moses** could not break through to achieve a complete reconciliation and the removal of this sin for the people. Instead, he had to step back and lead the people into that Word and state which had been spoken within him—namely, into the Word of the Law. This Law was given to them from **Sinai** and, in their state of nature and the flesh <term>Stand der Natur</term> <note>In this mystical context, the "state of nature" refers to humanity's fallen condition, governed by physical senses and selfish desires rather than spiritual enlightenment.</note>, it produced nothing but burning fire, darkness, and thunder—sheer fear and terror. Even **Moses**, reacting to what appeared to him, said: 
> *I am entirely terrified and full of trem-*
> *bling!*
<note>original: "Ich bin ganz erschrocken und voll Zitterens!" This is a reference to Hebrews 12:21, describing the terror of the Israelites and Moses at the foot of Mount Sinai.</note>

For they should have survived that time of testing—the forty days and powers of light—to enlighten their understanding and bring them to a living knowledge and a pure, unerring vision of **God**. They also faced the forty nights (or the powers of darkness working against those days) meant for the denial and killing of themselves <term>Verläugnung</term> <note>The "killing of the self" or abnegation is a central theme in Pietist mysticism, involving the surrender of the individual ego to God's will.</note> and all their own sensory life, self-will, falsehood, and lying powers. If they had endured this with true, faithful, and firm faith, then that very same Word—which in their state of nature worked as a curse, death, and condemnation—would have become pure light, life, love, peace, and joy with **God**. 

Previously, because of their sensory perception, they could not recognize or grasp the super-essential **God** <note>original: "überwesentlichen GOtt." A term used to describe God as being beyond all created substance or human categories of "being."</note> in His simplicity and purity as He is. Instead, they had to learn to recognize Him through various figures, images, and in many parts and ways, as these were formed in their imagination and senses through the glory of the angels under whom they stood. Yet, **Christ Jesus** would have been revealed and transfigured in them as their sole Mediator and Redeemer if they had faithfully followed him through the trial of self-denial—through true humiliation, humility, obedience, and dying to the self. He has become far more excellent than the angels and has inherited a much better name than they, so that they might have recognized **God** in a far higher quality of glory, and [lived] with Him in...

<meta>This page continues the theological explanation of why the Israelites were placed under the Law rather than entering directly into a spiritual kingdom. It contrasts the "state of transfiguration" they could have achieved with the "carnal" reality they fell into after the sin of the Golden Calf.</meta>

<page-num>90</page-num>

...could be united with **Christ**, just like the **angels** themselves. Thus, in the state of transfiguration <term>Stand der Verklärung</term> <note>A state of spiritual radiance or "glorification" where the soul reflects God's light.</note> of the majesty of **God** within them, they could have driven out all their enemies and the peoples of nature and the kingdom of darkness before them. They could have raised the **land of Canaan** into paradisiacal glory and virtue, and thereafter filled the whole earth through spiritual multiplication with their clarified seed <note>original: "clarificirten Saamen." In mystical thought, this refers to a purified spiritual lineage resulting from the "new birth."</note> of the new birth, and so live in an eternal rest and peace with **God**. 

But now, since they did not endure the trial but instead fell into terrible sin, the living Word—which **God** spoke in **Moses**—became to them again a curse, death, and damnation. And just as all the peoples of nature are subjected to certain orders of the **angels** and their princes (the archangels and principalities), so they too fell again under the angelic powers. Because of their carnal mind <note>A mindset focused on physical desires and worldly things rather than spiritual truth.</note>, they could be governed and led in no other way than by the hand or the service of the **angels**. They had to perceive **God’s** wisdom and nature as if through a telescope and a mirror, in various riddles, figures, images, veils, and coverings. 

And because they wanted to have gods who walked before their faces <note>A reference to the Israelites asking Aaron to make them gods to lead them in Exodus 32:1.</note>, they were given purely external and sensory perceptions <term>Vorwürffe</term> <note>In this period, "Vorwürffe" refers to objects presented to the senses or the mind's eye for contemplation.</note>, both of **God** and of divine service. Therefore, they could be led into no other land than an earthly one, and brought to no other rest than a carnal rest. And this is precisely the Day of the **Old Testament**, in which **God** visited and punished their sin, as the text reports and as that which follows thereafter sufficiently testifies.

To touch upon only one thing or another in passing—since explaining this mystery in all its circumstances would require a massive book—the following chapter contains a very thoughtful passage:

->Chapter XXXIII, 1-6.<-

> **Jehovah** spoke to **Moses**: Go, go down from here, you and the people...

<meta>This page continues the spiritual commentary on Exodus 33. On this page, the author interprets Moses’s act of pitching his tent outside the camp as a necessary spiritual withdrawal from the worldly self and the "apostate" community of the flesh to encounter God face-to-face.</meta>

<page-num>93</page-num>

**Jehovah** spoke to **Moses** face to face, as a man would speak to his neighbor. And he returned again to the camp; **Joshua**, the son of **Nun**, the youth, did not depart from the tent.

Dear reader, what does it mean that **Moses**—the mediator and army leader of this people, in whom **God** glorified Himself face to face—pitches the tent outside the camp and removes himself from all of **Israel**? It means that he can no longer remain in the fellowship of this apostate people, nor can he reach union with **God** there. Indeed, everyone who wishes to search with their desire into the high nature of **God** in the name of **Jehovah**, and who desires to be reconciled and united with **God**, must go out and distance themselves from their own tent of flesh and blood <note>original: "Hütten aus Fleisch und Blut." The author uses "tent" or "hut" as a metaphor for the physical body and the earthly ego.</note>, from their friends and brothers, and even from the whole congregation of **Israel**. 

O wonderful mystery! They had the promise just as much as we do: where two or three are united in the name of **Jehovah**, He will be in their midst. Here were over six hundred thousand people physically assembled, also incorporated and united in a covenant with **God** through **baptism** and **spiritual food** and **drink** <note>This is a reference to 1 Corinthians 10:2–4, where the Apostle Paul interprets the crossing of the Red Sea as baptism and the manna/water as spiritual food/drink.</note>; but **Jehovah** was not among them. Instead, whoever wanted to find Him had to go out from their tent of flesh and blood and from the whole congregation of **Israel**, removing themselves from all their fellowship. For inwardly, they were all alienated from the spiritual **citizenship** of **Israel** through the alienation of their hearts from the true, living **God**.

Did some from among the people follow **Moses** into the tent?

The text says: When **Moses** went out, they looked after him from behind. Because they would not follow him through self-denial and by going out from themselves, they could not [look him in the] fa-

The bright radiance of **God's** majesty, which was glorified in Moses outside the camp, appeared to their blinded, coarse carnal senses as a mist and clouds. What was a fiery light and clarity to Moses appeared to them as obscurity and darkness. And when the **Lord** glorified His word in Moses, illuminating His holy will within him, and Moses wished to speak and radiate that word from his own mouth and face into their hearts and minds, they nonetheless captured the meaning of the Spirit in carnal images, and the clarity of the divine will in dark riddles and figures.

This rock is precisely the one who followed—**Christ**; and the space and the hollow in which one can behold **God** unhindered are **Christ’s** wounds, primarily in his side. Within these, one can reach living knowledge through faithful following in suffering and obedience, and through the mortification <term>Abtödtung: the spiritual process of dying to the ego or old self</term> of oneself in the power of **Christ’s** death, one attains the true union and contemplation of **God**. It is very significant that it was previously written of **Moses** that he spoke with **God** face to face, but now he can and must learn to recognize **God** only in his "back parts" <note>original: "Hindertheil" — referring to the "after-effects" or "back" of God as seen in Exodus 33:23</note>; that is, from those things that have already passed by, in dark figures and images.

<meta>This page continues the interpretation of Exodus 34. The previous page discussed Moses’s physical and spiritual prostration before God’s glory. This page transitions into Moses's plea for God to dwell within the people and provides a mystical critique of outward religious ceremonies.</meta>

<page-num>100</page-num>
<header>(: ❦ :) 100 (: ❦ :)</header>

<margin>verse 9.</margin> And he said: "Dear Lord, if I have then found grace in your eyes, my Lord, then let the Lord go in our innermost! For this is a stiff-necked people; so you will forgive these our iniquities and these our sins, and you will take us up as a peculiar inheritance." <note>original: "eigenthumlichen Erb." This refers to the biblical concept of God's chosen people as His private possession or "treasure."</note>

We might indeed proceed further in the text of Moses, and demonstrate from it how the people of Israel, after this shameful idolatry <note>referring to the Golden Calf</note>, so miserably troubled <note>original: "turbiret," from the Latin turbare, meaning disturbed or thrown into disorder</note> and coarsened their mind <term>Gemüth: the inner soul, disposition, or seat of the emotions</term>, that they grasped everything—which the living Word had worked inwardly in Moses and presented to them for following and for seizing in faith—according to their carnal sense in nothing but coarse images, figures, and shadows. They formed God’s living, inwardly-working Word into outward gestures, and thus never arrived at the living truth and the true essence in Christ Jesus, who nonetheless followed them inwardly in a spiritual manner. From this, the entire outward service of the letter and the ceremonies of the Old Testament arose; and it is not actually the true and absolute command and working of the inner Word, but rather first formed itself by chance <note>that is, incidentally or as a concession to human weakness</note> in the carnal mind of the Israelites.

We can see a living example of this in outward Christianity, where our dear Lord sought—through His inwardly-working Word and Spirit—first to drive the entire service of the letter along with all outward business <term>Gewerb: the "industry" or mechanical performance of religious duties</term> of godliness out of the hearts of men, and thereafter to erase it from the outside as well through the terrible devastation <note>original: "Verhergung," an archaic form for the destruction of the city in 70 AD</note> of Jerusalem and the Temple. In its place, He sought to establish worship in spirit and in truth within us, to which He also directed, enticed, drawn, and driven all people with His entire teaching and life, suffering, death, and resurrection, in the demonstration of the Spirit and the power of God. But because the spirit-filled teaching of Christianity [was received] not into empty and spiritually-minded,

<meta>catchword: but [German: son- / sondern]</meta>

<margin>verse 9</margin> And he said: My Lord, if I have found favor in your eyes, then let the Lord go in our innermost <note>original: "in unserem inwendigsten"; a reference to Exodus 34:9, where Moses asks God to dwell in the midst of the people</note>! because this is a stiff-necked people; so you will forgive our iniquities and our sins, and take us up as your own inheritance.

We might indeed continue further in the text of Moses and prove from it how the people of Israel, after this shameful **idolatry**, had their minds so miserably troubled and coarsened that they grasped everything which the living Word had worked inwardly in Moses—and which he had presented to them for following and for grasping in faith—according to their fleshly sense in nothing but crude images, figures, and shadows. They fashioned God's living, inwardly-working Word into external gestures, and thus never reached the living truth and the true essence in **Christ Jesus**, who nevertheless followed them in a spiritual manner. From this, the entire external **service of the letter** <note>The "service of the letter" refers to a religious life based on following written rules and external rituals rather than inner spiritual life</note> and the ceremonies of the **Old Testament** arose. This was not actually the true and absolute command and working of the inner Word, but rather formed itself only incidentally within the fleshly mind of the Israelites.

We can see a living example of this in external **Christianity**, where our dear Lord sought first to drive the entire service of the letter, along with all external business of godliness, out of the hearts of men through his inwardly-working Word and Spirit. Afterward, he sought to abolish it from the outside through the terrible devastation of **Jerusalem** and the Temple, and in its place to establish the worship in spirit and in truth within us. To this end, he directed, enticed, drawn, and driven all people with his entire teaching and life, suffering, death, and resurrection, in the demonstration of the Spirit and the power of God. ## But how does spiritual and physical fornication arise from image-worship and idolatry?

<margin>1 Corinthians 10:8</margin>
Idolatry and image-worship—whether it be coarse or subtle, fleshly or spiritual, external or internal, in all its forms—is a root and mother not only of hidden malice, but also of the most shameful sins and vices, especially of physical fornication and adultery itself. Therefore, Paul immediately follows the warning that we should not become idolaters with: "Neither let us commit fornication, as some of them committed, and fell in one day three and twenty thousand!" <note>The author is quoting the Bible to show that throughout history, whenever people turned to idols (false images of God), sexual immorality followed shortly after.</note> And it is no wonder that after spiritual fornication <note>meaning unfaithfulness to God in the heart</note> and adultery, physical and fleshly fornication also breaks out. For when the human mind has formed some pleasant and agreeable image, the impure heart is immediately ignited by it in impure lust, and the image is seized in the lecherous heat of self-love; this then immediately expresses itself in sensual feelings, and the flames of impure natural love break out, one against the other. Thus, when a God-seeking soul is first awakened to the **new birth** and is of a somewhat more violent, fiery, and active **temperament** <note>original: "complexion"; in the 17th century, this referred to a person’s biological and psychological makeup based on the four humors</note>, the fiery nature is commonly awakened at the same time as the inner seed of the Word. Then the desire becomes very intense, and the hunger so great, that the soul seizes everything without distinction,

...grasps whatever it can seize: there it reads and hears, there it runs and searches and asks and looks for everything it finds tasty enough to satisfy its desire. It fashions, forms, and impresses itself into them according to all its natural powers—one way today, another tomorrow. All these images are no better than straw in the fire, serving only to ignite the poor soul in impure love, until finally the flames burst out—appearing first as "brotherly love" in social interactions, and finally breaking out into physical, fleshly union. 

I will say nothing now of the supposed "spiritual procreation" <note>The author is likely critiquing certain radical religious groups of the time who claimed to "spiritually beget" followers, a practice that often led to sexual scandals.</note> where some imagine themselves in a certain state of being "fathers" or "mothers" and grasp the <term>idol</term> <note>original Latin: "idolum"</note> or image of the person they wish to "give birth" to within their fervent imagination. Not to mention the shameful gestures they commonly perform toward those they wish to "give birth" to—following the impulse of their coarse mental imagery—while boasting of a special "paradisiacal delight." This pen would not dare touch upon such things if this <term>fornication</term> <note>original: "Hurerey"</note> had not broken out openly among us, causing great offense and terrible blasphemy of the truth before the entire world. Thus, all such spiritual and physical fornications are nothing other than outflows from the main source of all filth, of both the flesh and the spirit: namely, from image-worship and idolatry.

However, so that we may use not only the examples of those Israelites of old, but also the sorrowful examples of today’s "Israelites" <note>Referring here to professing Christians or members of the author's own religious community.</note> as a faithful warning, we wish to investigate: 
1. The occasion of that fornication. 
2. The fornication itself.
3. What followed after it.

# What was the cause of that fornication?

The cause is found in the 4th Book of Moses, chapter 22 <note>This refers to the Book of Numbers, chapter 22.</note>. When Moses / the servant of the Lord and mediator of the people of Israel / had obtained some reconciliation and peace with God for this people, who had been weakened by idolatry / he took up the counsel and power of the Lord to move forward with the people out of the wilderness / at least into the earthly Canaan. And / because they had lost and gambled away the **Sword of the Spirit** <note>A reference to Ephesians 6:17, meaning the Word of God used as a spiritual weapon.</note> and the power of the living, internally-powerful-working Word to drive their enemies out before them / he intended to exterminate them with the worldly sword through the assistance and protection of the angels / as they then actually defeated the Canaanites and Amorites / and reached the plains of Moab. 

Now when Balak / the son of Zippor / the king of the Midianites / saw what Israel had done to the Amorites / he and all the people feared the children of Israel greatly / and spoke to the elders of the Midianites: "Now this congregation will lick up everything surrounding us / just as the ox licks up the green grass of the field." For the dread of the God of Jacob held them in such a way that they well perceived / that / before they could weaken the spiritual and heavenly power of the protective Angel of this people through the opposing power of darkness and spiritual wickedness / they would not be able to drive away the children of Israel with their fleshly, external power. 

Therefore, they attempted through false **magic** <note>original Latin: "magiam"</note> and **incantation** <note>original Latin: "incantation"</note> or conjuring / which was powerful among the heathens at that time / to weaken the people / and to bring some curse or ban from their God upon them. Therefore, Balak sent to Balaam / the son of Beor / and had it said to him: "Behold / a people has come out of Egypt; behold / it covers the eye of this earth / and it dwells opposite me. Commonly, these people appear in the highest disguise of spirituality and outward humility. Indeed, they rarely approach man to man or woman to woman; rather, when a man raises himself to the status of teacher and multiplier <note>original: *Lehr- und Mehr-stand*; a reference to those who assume authority to teach and seek to "multiply" their followers or the church</note>, he usually addresses himself to female persons, while a woman addresses herself to male persons. In the beginning, they speak very few words, fearing they might betray the wickedness of their hearts. Instead, they first attempt—like Balaam <note>original: *Bileam*; a biblical prophet who was tempted to curse Israel for reward</note> from the heights and through the power of Baal <note>original: *Baals*; a Canaanite deity often used in this context to represent false or demonic power</note>—to seize lustful souls (for detached spirits <note>referring to those who have truly separated themselves from worldly desires</note> they cannot easily grasp) into their intensely-acting imagination <note>original: *imagination*; in this context, it refers to a potent, creative spiritual force capable of influencing others</note>, and to probe the nature of those souls and their inner desires.

As young, inexperienced hearts easily open themselves out of evil lust or natural curiosity, it is all the easier for these sorcerous spirits to instill and awaken some sensation—either of anxiety, if they perceive a conflicting object within, or of joy and sweetness, if they harmonize and are in agreement—through the magical driving <note>original: *Eintrieb*</note> of their will and craving. ## What is the third archetype of Israel’s deviations, which Paul presents to us as a warning?

Let us also not tempt Christ, just as some of them tempted him, and were destroyed by serpents! <note>A reference to 1 Corinthians 10:9, which looks back to the events in Numbers 21:5–6 where the Israelites complained about their lack of food and water, leading God to send "fiery serpents" among them.</note> The Apostle Paul does not arrange the archetypes <note>original: "Fürbilder." In this context, it refers to "types" or historical patterns that serve as prophetic models or warnings for future generations.</note> of Israel’s temptations and deviations in the chronological order in which they occurred and were recorded in the books of Moses. Instead, he follows the various degrees and stages of their falling away. He places the **Calf-Worship** <note>The idolatry of the Golden Calf (Exodus 32).</note> first, as it is the greatest sin and the cause of all the others. This is because the temptation that follows is nothing other than a fruit and effect of that coarse, animalistic misrepresentation of the invisible **GOD**. 

For when a person forms an image of **GOD** within their own coarse, animalistic mind, and serves Him only with the powers of the senses and of their own flesh and blood, they do not expect a spiritual or heavenly reward. Rather, they look for a fleshy and earthly reward: in eating and drinking, in sensory delights, and in earthly luxury. And if such rewards do not follow this impure, coarse worship of **GOD**, then nothing remains but grumbling and impatience, lamentation and annoyance. 

This can be seen here in "Israel according to the flesh," <note>A Pauline term (1 Corinthians 10:18) distinguishing those who are merely biological descendants or focused on outward rituals from those who are spiritually "Israel."</note> who formed for themselves an animalistic God and served that animalistic God in a carnal way, through flesh and blood. They did this so that they might enjoy an external, fleshy rest, and feed the "coarse beast" of the fleshy-minded human in an earthly and sensory Canaan <note>The Promised Land, here interpreted metaphorically as a place of worldly satisfaction rather than spiritual peace.</note> according to all its lusts and desires. For those who live according to the flesh set their minds on the things of the flesh; but those who live according to the Spirit, on the things of the Spirit. <note>A direct quotation of Romans 8:5.</note> However, because the carnal mind is an enmity or hostility toward **GOD**, and produces nothing but death, the Lord does not lead His people according to the desires of their flesh, but according to the mind of His Spirit, which is life and

<summary>This page analyzes the Apostle Paul's use of Old Testament failures as spiritual warnings, arguing that idolatry (Calf-Worship) stems from a "carnal mind" that seeks earthly rewards rather than spiritual life.</summary>

and peace: which, however, in the fleshly-minded person brings nothing but grumbling, adversity, and disgust, as **Israel** did here. When they arrived at the land of their fleshly brothers, the **Edomites** <note>The Edomites were descendants of Esau, the brother of Jacob (Israel). The text calls them "fleshly brothers" to highlight their shared ancestry but different spiritual paths.</note>, they wished to pass through the fertile dwelling-place of **Esau**; partly because the path through **Edom** was closer and straighter into **Canaan**, and partly because it was also more comfortable and the highway was better. Human reason might wonder whether **Moses** had not requested passage through the **Edomites'** land according to **GOD’s** express command? And since they refused them this, why did they not then defend themselves, but instead retreated and took such a long detour? The text mentions no express command from **GOD**, and the embassy to **Edom** was not carried out in the name of the **Lord**, but in the name of **Israel** itself. It is also credible that **Moses**, as a prophet of **GOD**, likely knew beforehand that it was not the **Lord’s** will for **Israel** to pass through **Edom**, and also knew that **Edom** would refuse the passage. But because he was aware of the people's malice—that if he led the people on such a frustrating detour before they saw that it was absolutely necessary, they would easily have cast all their anger, impatience, and resentment upon him as their leader along the way—so it undoubtedly happened according to the wise counsel and guidance of **GOD**. For if the children of **Israel** had seen the fleshly rest and the richness <note>original: "Fettigkeit," literally "fatness," referring to the fertility and abundance of the land.</note> of the earth which **Esau** had as his dwelling, they might have—if not entirely [settled] therein—

...encamped, yet according to their fleshly mind they were held back and were difficult to move forward.

Because they now had to bypass the land of the **Edomites**, they journeyed from **Mount Hor** along the path of the edge of the sea, or along the shore of the sea, which some call the **Sea of Reeds** <note>original: *Schilff-Meer*, the traditional German name for the Red Sea</note>, because reeds usually grow at the edge of the sea. They did this in order to bypass the land of **Edom**, and the life or the soul of the people was "shortened" on this path—that is, they became vexed and impatient. This was partly because there are no paths that cause more fatigue or are more annoying than detours; and partly because the path through the reeds on the seashore may have been a dangerous, difficult, swampy, and marshy way. Therefore, the text very thoughtfully notes that the life of the people became "short;” not only because the outward exertion made the people so weary that many died all the sooner, but because it was a path that moved the very mind and soul of the human being to impatience and made them quick to anger. Thus, it was not as small a trial as one might imagine.

And the people spoke against **GOD** and against **Moses**: "Why have you made us come up out of **Egypt** to die in the wilderness? For there is no bread and no water, and our life becomes 'shortened' <note>meaning their souls were exhausted or disgusted</note> by this very miserable bread." They blamed the **Manna**—the bread from heaven—for the fact that they were perishing in the desert and growing weary on the journey. They believed that if they had earthly bread and water, they would not become so exhausted, and their bodies would not waste away in the wilderness. O, wicked blindness!

Regarding the explanation of this history, we gladly grant everyone the freedom to believe according to the translation of *Luther* <note>Martin Luther</note> and other scholars: that those serpents which bit *Israel* were a kind of physical "burning serpent," and that *Moses* raised nothing other than a material serpent cast from ore. While they did physically bite the children of *Israel* in the form of poisonous burning serpents, they most especially poisoned the souls of the *Israelites* with burning temptations and trials, wounding them in the depths of their hearts.

Such people were corrupted in the wilderness by the serpents and swallowed up in temporal and eternal death. For this property of God in a soul ignited in fury is precisely the "worm that does not die," and this burning enmity against God is the "fire that is not quenched." 

Some *Israelites*, who had not conceived such an adversity against God and *Christ*, but who were indeed bitten by the serpents on that vexing and difficult path and were tempted toward enmity and loathing, did not fully consent to the temptation. It transitions from the historical narrative of the Israelites to an internal, spiritual application for the reader, focusing on the "new birth" within the soul.</meta>

...powerfully-working Advocate <note>original: *Fürsprecher*</note> Jesus in the name of Jehovah and in the power of the eternal love of God, he was so thoroughly fired by the high property <term>Eigenschafft: a central term in Boehmenist mysticism referring to a specific quality or attribute of the divine or natural world</term> of God that he became like an angel burning in the love of God, a divine seraph. And in such power, as an excellent Magus <note>In this context, a "Magus" is not a sorcerer, but a spiritual initiate who understands how to work with divine forces</note>, he raised up the serpent-treader <note>original: *Schlangen-treter*; a reference to Christ as the one who crushes the head of the serpent as promised in Genesis 3:15</note> in all souls who grasped him with the eyes of faith. He appeased and reconciled all the burning powers and the souls ignited with the hellish flames of God's wrath with the love of God, and banished the poisonous devil's brood from the hearts of the salvation-hungry Israelites. 

But so that the still-sensual <note>meaning those focused on physical senses rather than spiritual reality</note> Israelites might also grasp this for their salvation, we will not deny that Moses, as a skillful Magus, set such a magical and auguric <note>original: *augurische*; relating to signs or symbolic observation</note> serpent before their eyes. This was done so that they might grasp the reconciling and healing grace and love of God from the image, through the eyes of faith, with true hunger into their essence and heart. Through this appeasing and in-working grace, their souls—ignited by the poison of grim wrath and adversity—would be calmed and strengthened into peace with God.

## But how does this archetype <note>original: *Fürbild*</note> apply to our warning?

O dear brothers! They tempted Christ in archetype and parable <note>original: *Gleichnuß*</note>; we tempt Him in essence and life. One may ask: How so? The entire Holy Scripture and all the testimonies of the Evangelists and Apostles unanimously point us to the fact that Christ Jesus wants to carry out the entire work of redemption through His Holy Spirit within us, just as truly and essentially—both in the killing of the old corrupt nature and in the rebirth of the new man—as He carried out and completed it for us in the days of His flesh through the Cross and death, Resurrection and Ascension.

It then transitions to a call for the reader to experience the 'new birth' and the work of Christ internally.</summary>

...also tempted him? Indeed, if we in these times—when the powers of malice have risen to their highest strength—would thoroughly examine and get to know ourselves, we would see how we have, as it were, completely "tempted out" **Christ** <note>original: *aus-versucht*; the author plays on the word "tempted" to suggest both testing Christ's patience and trying to "extract" or "drain" him for selfish ends</note>, and drained him with our temptations and evil desires. For soon there will be no sin, abomination, or malice committed by us that we do not seek to cloak with **Christ**, or even attribute to the working of his **Holy Spirit**. If someone is inflamed in the impure passion of harlot-love, it is called the "fervent brotherly love of **Christ**"; if someone lives according to the will and lusts of the flesh, it is called "Christian liberty" <note>A reference to the theological concept of *libertas christiana*, which the author argues is being abused as a license to sin</note>; if someone walks in the greatest disorder, it is said to be nothing but the "guidance of **GOD**." 

Indeed, when someone falls into open sin and vice, which the honorable world is accustomed to punish according to the laws of nature, they do not even wish to submit to such proper punishment and discipline of nature for **Christ's** sake. Thus, one can soon devise no sin, and practice it spiritually or physically, which does not parade under the name of the virtues of **JESUS**, and present itself as a grace of **GOD** in some praise from the letter of the Scripture. Not to mention the "spiritualities of wickedness" <note>A reference to "spiritual wickedness in high places" from Ephesians 6:12</note> in the celestial realms, by which many a poor soul is deceived against their knowledge under the appearance of great powers of the pure wisdom of **GOD**. Does this not mean to tempt and spy upon **Christ**, in whom the whole fullness of the Godhead dwells bodily?

[He] beams the flames of His love through divine magic <note>original: "Magiam"; in this mystical context, "magic" refers to the transformative spiritual power of God that acts upon the soul, rather than sorcery.</note> into the heart for so long, until the heart itself grasps that same quality once again in humility and obedience, and is interpenetrated <note>original: "inqualiret"; a technical term in Jacob Boehme’s mysticism meaning to be imbued with or to work in unison with a specific spiritual quality.</note> with pure love in God. > **Lord Jesus!** look and see how the serpents of burning desires and carnal lusts have poisoned your Israel, and how those who boast of being members of your body are inflamed by the heat of many poisonous temptations; yes, how the cold gangrene <note>original: "kalte Brand"; literally "cold fire," a historical term for gangrene or necrosis. Yes, ignite us with your fire-flaming eyes, so that from now on we may burn in your pure divine love, and through this fire, completely burn out all the powers of self-love and carnal lusts, and love and praise you eternally as pure seraphim and hearts flaming with love!

### What was the cause of the murmuring?

...blood; they shall become heaps of ash! Concerning us, dear brothers! we who have perhaps departed from those external temples of religions and sects, let us not be without a temple! ...and there creeps in the darkness such a secret, weakening magical power, that even the wisest virgins with full lamps of faith fall into slumber. <note>A reference to the Parable of the Ten Virgins in Matthew 25, where the "lamps" represent spiritual preparedness and faith.</note>

But shall we, in the face of all this, let our courage sink, give up the work of sanctification, and turn back into despair and unbelief? Far be it! So much the more let us lift up our heads—albeit in humility and faith—because so many infallible signs assure us of the coming of our Lord! For these days are shortened for the sake of God’s elect: and the more cruelly the powers of darkness rage, the sooner that midnight shall break in, in which the Bridegroom shall come, and the night—in which the Lord shall be transfigured in His wisdom and truth—shall begin. For we know that the **Day of the Lord comes like a thief in the night, on which the heavens shall pass away with a great noise, and the elements shall be ignited by heat and dissolved, and the earth and the works that are upon it shall burn up.** Since then all these things shall be dissolved, what manner of persons ought you to be in **holy conduct and godliness; and waiting for, yes, even hastening, the coming of the day of God, on which the heavens shall burn with fire and be dissolved, and the elements shall be ignited with heat and melt!** <note>This paragraph is a close rendering of 2 Peter 3:10-12, emphasizing the apocalyptic expectation of the author's community.</note>

There is nothing that leads a God-seeking soul more safely through the cruel, raging <term>uproar</term> <note>original: *Turbam*; a Latin term for a chaotic crowd or commotion, here referring to spiritual distraction.</note> of the powers of darkness and temptations in these days than faithfulness and truth; the "Yes and Amen" of God set into a faithful and honest heart with true faith and pure love; a heart that constantly searches itself through the in-working light of grace, and in all faithfulness and honesty separates itself—without delay and rationalizing deliberation—from all discovered and found <term>self-will</term> <note>original: *Eigenheit*; in Pietist theology, this refers to the "ego" or the tendency of the soul to act for its own sake rather than for God.</note>. It matters not whether this self-will is found in evil desire or self-loving intentions, where <term>selfhood</term> <note>original: *Selbstheit*; the state of being focused on the self.</note> always hides itself: within itself or outside of itself; in bodily or spiritual creatures; in brotherhood and kinship; according to the flesh or according to the spirit; in earthly or heavenly things. It matters not whether the soul may feel and recognize God within itself, or may sit in darkness and affliction of the mind without any feeling. And thus, let one daily learn to deny oneself, whom one...

<summary>The author encourages believers to remain vigilant despite spiritual exhaustion, citing the approaching "Day of the Lord" as a reason for holy living and rigorous self-examination to root out "selfhood."</summary>

<keywords>Sanctification, Day of the Lord, Parable of the Ten Virgins, Apocalypticism, Self-will, Spiritual Vigilance, Self-examination</keywords>

<meta>This page concludes the author’s introductory exhortation, which began on page 174. It transitions from a warning about the world's distractions—described on the previous page as a "raging uproar"—to a fervent prayer for divine protection and the sanctification of the text itself.</meta>

[the things one] sees and feels; to hate [the visible], but to learn to love the invisible God, whom one does not see, according to His height and depth, breadth and length, until reaching the eternal rest and contentment of one's heart! <note>A reference to the dimensions of divine love described in Ephesians 3:18.</note>

But You, O Lord! You who hold the seven stars firmly in Your right hand, and who walk in the midst of the seven golden lampstands! <note>These images are drawn from the opening vision of the Book of Revelation (1:16, 2:1).</note> You alone are the one who can break through all the seven powers of darkness and enlighten our hearts with Your Holy Spirit. Appear then to Your scattered Israel as a **pillar of fire** in this encroaching night! You, O Lord, have been tempted in all things just as we are, and in forty days You conquered all hellish powers—both in the kingdom of this world and in flesh and blood—with great faithfulness and wisdom: yes, You fought with the prince of darkness even unto Your death, and through death You brought to nothing the one who had the power over death—that is, the devil! Therefore, O Lord, You <term>Captain of our salvation</term> <note>original: *Herzog unserer Seligkeit*; while "Herzog" literally means Duke, in this theological context it refers to Christ as the pioneer or author of salvation (Hebrews 2:10).</note>, may You not only impress these prototypes of ancient Israel upon our hearts as a warning, but also pour in and seal Your divine power and Your Holy Spirit for our victory! 

Though the dragon steps forth from the abyss with all his angels and powers of destruction, pouring out his venom like a flood to seduce Your **poor little flock**, upon whom the ends of the world's course have come, may You nevertheless pour out the streams of Your healing grace—the spirit of wisdom, of living truth and faithfulness, yes, all the powers of Your immeasurable love from Your deep wounds into our hearts—all the more because the time is short, the danger great, the temptation powerful, and we are still so weak, wretched, and poor! To You, O Lord, I commend—in the cooperation of Your Holy Spirit—my weak presentation of this **Apostolic Warning**, which I have composed in letters from a well-meaning heart for the awakening and encouragement of myself and my brothers who stumble, are tempted, and deviate alongside me! You, Lord, have the spirit and life, the wisdom and power, to bless the dead letter for living edification, and to strengthen these powerless words for the true sanctification of us all! To You alone shall all blessing be hallowed; all praise, honor, glory, power, and wisdom be given and 

->offered up in spirit and truth, Amen!<-

### Decorative Illustration

->**Floral Trellis Pattern**<-

This page features a decorative patterned paper of a style often used for book covers or endpapers in the eighteenth and nineteenth centuries. The design consists of a diagonal lattice or **trellis** made of grey, **thorny branches**. 

At the points where the branches intersect, and placed at regular intervals along the stems, are repeating motifs of small, reddish-pink **flower buds** and green **trilobed leaves**. <note>The "trilobed" leaf—a leaf divided into three parts—is often used in religious manuscripts as a subtle symbol of the Trinity.</note> The pattern is set against a warm, cream-colored background.

This type of "block-printed" paper provides a moment of visual repose for the reader, separating the introductory exhortations from the historical and theological accounts that follow. The inclusion of thorns alongside new buds may mirror the manuscript's themes of spiritual struggle and eventual growth.

<summary>This page contains a decorative trellis pattern featuring thorny branches, flower buds, and trilobed leaves, serving as a visual transition or endpaper.</summary>

<keywords>floral pattern, trellis, thorns, endpaper, bookbinding, trilobed leaves, decorative art</keywords>
